GENERAL PROVISIONS
Sec. 90.01 CRUELTY.
   No person shall cruelly treat any animal in the town in any way. Any person
who inhumanely beats, overloads, underfeeds, or abandons any animal shall be
guilty of a violation of this section.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.02 DANGEROUS ANIMALS.
   It shall be unlawful to permit any dangerous or vicious animal of any kind
to run at large in the town. Exhibitions or parades of animals which are ferae
natured (wild natured) in the eyes of the law may be conducted only upon
securing a permit from the Town Clerk.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.03 NOISES.
   It shall be unlawful to harbor or keep any animal which disturbs the peace
by loud noises at any time of the day or night.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.04 STRAYS.
   It shall be unlawful to permit any cattle, horses, swine, sheep, goats, or
poultry to run at large in the town. Any such animal running at large in any
public place in the town shall be impounded in the manner provided in Sec.
90.20 to
90.42. It shall be further unlawful to picket or tie any such animal in the
streets of the town for the purpose of grazing or feeding.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.05 KILLING DANGEROUS ANIMALS.
   The members of the Sheriff’s office or any other person in the town are
authorized to kill any dangerous animal when it is necessary for the protection
of any person or property.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.06 DISEASED ANIMALS.
   No domestic animal afflicted with a contagious disease or an infectious
disease shall be allowed to run at large, or to be exposed in any public place
whereby the health of human or beast may be affected, nor shall such diseased
animal be shipped or removed from the premises of the owner thereof, except
under the supervision of the Town Police or County Veterinarian. It is hereby
made the duty of the Town Police to secure such disposition of any diseased
animal and such treatment of affected premises as to prevent the communication
or spread of contagion or infection, except in cases where the State
Veterinarian is empowered to act.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.07 HOUSING.
   No person shall cause or allow any stable or place where any animal is or
may be kept to become unclean, or unwholesome, and it shall be unlawful to keep
any live swine or pigs anywhere in the town.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
DOMESTIC PETS
Sec. 90.20 INTENT AND PURPOSES.
   It is the intent and purpose of this subchapter to promote responsible pet
ownership, to protect animals from neglect and abuse, to protect residents from
annoyance, injury, and property damage from animals, and to provide for
education in the responsible ownership of pets.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.21 DEFINITIONS.
For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
ADMINISTRATOR. A veterinarian licensed by the state and appointed pursuant to
the provisions of this subchapter, or his or her duly authorized
representative.
ANIMAL. Any vertebrate member of the animal kingdom, excluding humans.
ANIMAL CONTROL CENTER. Any pound, lot, premises, and/or building maintained by
or under contract with the county for the care and custody of animals.
BOARD. The Town Board.
OWNER. Any person having a right of property in a dog or other animal, or who
keeps or harbors a dog or other animal, or who has it in his or her care, or
acts as its custodian, or who knowingly permits a dog or other domestic animal
to remain on or about any premises occupied by him or her.
RESTRAINT. Under the control of the owner or his or her agent by leash, or
within a vehicle being driven or parked.
RUNNING AT LARGE. To be free of restraint beyond the boundaries of the premises
of the owner.
STRAY. Any animal found running at large which is not secured by a leash, or
not confined within the real property limits of its owner.
VACCINATION. Protection provided against rabies by inoculation with anti-rabies
vaccine as approved by the Department of Agriculture of the state.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.22 ANIMAL CONTROL CENTERS.
   There are hereby created animal control centers, which shall be established
in such number and at such locations as may be designated by the Board.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.23 VACCINATION AGAINST RABIES.
   Every owner of a dog four months or more of age, not confined at all times
to an enclosed area, shall cause such dog to be vaccinated against rabies by a
licensed veterinarian at such intervals as may be established by regulations
pursuant to the State Animal Control Act. Evidence of such rabies vaccination
shall be entered on a certificated, the form of which shall be approved by the
Board and which shall be signed by the licensed veterinarian administering the
vaccine.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.24 REGISTRATION OF DOGS.
   Every owner of a dog four months of age or more shall cause such dog to be
registered and shall pay an annual fee for such dog to the office of the Animal
Control Administrator of the county. The annual registration fee shall be set
by the County Animal Control. Each registered dog will be issued a registration
certificate and a registration tag. The tag shall be attached to a collar or
harness worn at all times by the dog for which the registration certificate and
tags have been issued, except when such dog is confined. When an owner fails to
register, his or her dog shall be subject to fines as set by Sec.
90.99.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.25 POSSESSION OF STRAY ANIMALS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person, without the knowledge and consent of
the owner, to retain possession of any stray animal for more than 48 hours
without first reporting such possession to the County Animal Control
Administrator and providing his or her name, address, a description of the
animal, the location of the animal, and a statement of the circumstances under
which possession of the animal was obtained.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see, Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.26 RUNNING AT LARGE.
   (1)   All dogs, regardless of the breed or size of the animal, shall be on a
leash at all times when outside of their containment or home.
   (2)   It shall be unlawful for any person to maintain a public nuisance by
permitting any dog to be outside when not under control by leash.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.27 SURRENDER OF ANIMALS.
   It shall be unlawful for any person who obtains possession of a stray animal
to refuse to surrender such animal to a County Animal Control Warden upon
demand.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.28 APPREHENSION OF DOGS.
   It shall be the duty of the County Animal Control Officer to apprehend any
dog found running at large.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.29 IMPOUNDMENT OF ANIMALS.
   It shall be the duty of the County Animal Control Officer to impound in an
animal control center in the county any animal over which possession is
obtained under the provisions of this subchapter.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.30 NOTICE OF IMPOUNDMENT.
   When any animal has been impounded in a county animal control center, the
Animal Control Officer shall give written notice to the owner, if known, at
such owner’s last known address.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.31 REDEMPTION OF ANIMALS.
   The owner of any animal impounded under the provisions of this subchapter
may redeem such animal upon complying with the following conditions:
   (1)   Presenting proof of current registration and vaccination of the
animal;
   (2)   Obtaining and paying for the registration and vaccination of the
animal, if required; and
   (3)   Paying for the boarding and care of the animal.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.32 DISPOSAL OF ANIMALS.
   The County Animal Control Officer may dispose of an impounded animal not
redeemed, no sooner than seven days after mailing notice to the owner or after
determining that the owner cannot be identified, by either:
   (1)   Placing the animal in an adoptive home;
   (2)   Selling the animal; or
   (3)   Destroying the animal in a humane manner.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.33 ADOPTION OF ANIMALS.
   Any person desiring to adopt an animal offered for adoption by the County
Animal Control Officer may do so upon such conditions and after the payment of
such expenses as the Animal Control Officer’s rules and regulations require.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.34 MAINTENANCE OF RECORDS.
   (1)   The County Animal Control Officer shall maintain records of all
animals apprehended and/or impounded.
   (2)   Such records shall be maintained for at least one year following
disposal of each animal and shall contain at least the following information:
      (A)   A complex description of the animal, including at least color,
size, sex, and a description of any tags;
      (B)   The manner and date of acquisition of the animal;
      (C)   The manner and date of disposal of the animal;
      (D)   The name and address of the person adopting or purchasing the
animal;
      (E)   A record of all fees and/or fines received in relation to the
animal; and
      (F)   The reason for disposal of the animal and the manner used.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.35 ENTRY UPON PRIVATE PROPERTY.
   The County Animal Control Officer shall have the authority to enter upon
private property at reasonable times and in a reasonable manner for the purpose
of carrying out the provisions of this subchapter.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.36 EDUCATION.
   The County Animal Control Officer shall promote and assist in programs for
education in responsible pet ownership in cooperation with civic groups,
professional organizations, and schools in the county.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.37 ENFORCEMENT.
   Actions to recover penalties for violations may be instituted by serving a
notice of violation upon the owner by the County Animal Control Officer or
Sheriff.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.38 SERVICE.
   Service of a notice of violation may be made by the Animal Control Officer
or the Sheriff by leaving a copy of the notice with the owner of the animal, or
by notice in a sealed envelope with postage fully prepaid and addressed to the
owner at his or her usual place of abode.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.39 DISPOSITION OF FEES AND FINES.
   Any fees and fines received under the provisions of this subchapter shall be
paid over to the Town Clerk.
(Ord. passed - -)
Sec. 90.40 KEEPING OF DOGS WITHIN THE TOWN LIMITS.
   All dogs, regardless of the breed or size of the animal, shall be ruled
under the conditions of this subchapter.
   (1)   There shall be a limit on the number of dogs per household. This limit
shall be set at two dogs per household.
   (2)   No dog runs or kennels shall be allowed to be in front of any
property.
   (3)   No dogs shall be leashed or tied in a manner that will allow the
animal to come within 15 feet of the public right-of-way, sidewalk, or
alleyway.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.41 KEEPING OF VICIOUS DOGS.
   (1)   Definition. For the purpose of this section, the following definition
applies unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      VICIOUS DOG.
      (1)   Any dog that, when unprovoked, bites or attacks a human being or
other animal on either public or private property shall be deemed VICIOUS.
      (2)   Any dog with a known, documented propensity, tendency, or
disposition to attack without provocation, to cause injury, or to otherwise
endanger the safety of human beings or domestic animals.
      (3)   Any dog which has been found to be a dangerous dog on two separate
occasions in this or any other jurisdiction, provided that such finding was not
based solely upon the breed of the dog.
      (4)   Any dog that has been found to be a VICIOUS DOG in any other
jurisdiction.
      (5)   No dog shall be deemed a VICIOUS DOG if it bites, attacks, or
menaces a trespasser on the property of its owner, or harms or menaces anyone
who has tormented or abused it, or is performing in the line of duty as a
professionally trained dog for law enforcement.
   (2)   Requirements for keeping dogs declared vicious.
      (A)   Any person owning or keeping a dog declared vicious must be in
compliance with the requirements of this section within 14 days of receipt of
notification of the declaration or within 14 days of the receipt of a decision
of an appeal upholding the vicious dog declaration. Any failure to comply with
the requirements of this section pertaining to the keeping of dog declared
vicious shall result in the impoundment of the dog and the dog being subject to
euthanasia. Failure to comply with this section may also result in fines, fees,
and court costs being assessed against the owner. Each failure to comply with a
provision of this section shall constitute a separate offense. Each day an
owner fails to comply with a provision of this section shall constitute a
separate offense.
      (B)   It shall be illegal for an owner or keeper of a vicious dog to
sell, foster, or give away the dog.
      (C)   The owner of a dog declared vicious shall contact the town law
enforcement chairperson within 14 days of receipt of the declaration, or within
14 days of the receipt of a decision of an appeal upholding the vicious dog
declaration, and arrange for an inspection of the premises where the dog
subject to said declaration is kept. It shall be illegal for any person to keep
or maintain any dog which has been found to be a vicious dog unless such dog is
at all times kept in an enclosure, as defined in this section.
      (D)   (1)   The only times that a vicious dog may be allowed out of an
enclosure are if it is necessary for the owner or keeper to obtain veterinary
care for the vicious dog or to comply with the order of a court of competent
jurisdiction. When taken out of the enclosure, said vicious dog must be
securely muzzled and restrained with a chain having a tensile strength of at
least 300 pounds and not exceeding three feet in length, and shall be under the
direct control and supervision of the owner or keeper of the vicious dog.
         (2)   Any dog which has been found to be a vicious dog and which is
not confined in an enclosure shall be impounded and may be subject to
euthanasia.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Statutory reference:
   Liability of owner of vicious dog for civil damages, see 510 ILCS 5/16-16
Sec. 90.42 KEEPING OF LIVESTOCK WITHIN THE TOWN LIMITS.
   (1)   For the purpose of this section, the following definition applies
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      LIVESTOCK. Swine, bovine, equine, goat, llamas, ostrich, and other
herding type animals.
   (2)   It shall be unlawful for the keeping of livestock within the town
limits, with the following exceptions: Any one residence will be allowed to
keep poultry with the limit of only six hens. No roosters will be allowed
within the town limits.
   (3)   (A)   Any persons keeping poultry must obtain a permit from the town.
Animals must be provided with adequate housing, houses, hutched pans, or other
enclosures wherein animals are kept shall have solid flooring for cleaning
purposes.
      (B)   Coops must be a minimum of 50 feet from any dwelling on adjacent
lots.
      (C)   Birds must be kept securely in an enclosed yard or pen at all
times.
      (D)   Poultry are permitted as pets for eggs. Animals cannot be kept for
the use of slaughter.
   (4)   Generally, it shall be unlawful for any person having authority to
control the use of any pen, shed, or stall for the keeping of poultry to become
unsanitary or inhumane for animals to be kept.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see Sec.
90.99
Sec. 90.99 PENALTY.
   (1)   Any person violating any of the provisions of Sec.
90.01 to
90.07 shall be fined in accordance with Sec.
10.99 for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
   (2)   Any person violating any provision of Sec.
90.20 to
90.42 shall be fined $100 for a first or second offense. Said fine may be paid
to the Town Clerk. For a third or subsequent offense, payment may not be made
to the Town Clerk, but must instead be determined by the Circuit Court, and
that fine shall not be less than $150 nor more than $200 for each offense.
(Ord. passed - -)

NUISANCES GENERALLY
Sec. 91.01 POLICY.
   (1)   Authority. Corporate authorities by 65 ILCS 5/1-2-5.
   (2)   Policy statement. It is the policy of the Board of Trustees to ensure
quality of life issues for the community.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008)
Sec. 91.02 NUISANCES DECLARED.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition applies unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   NUISANCE.
      (A)   Any business, establishment, building, or premises, or part
thereof, within the town limits which is detrimental or injurious to the public
health or comfort of persons in the vicinity thereof.
      (B)   Any stable or place in which swine, cattle, or other animals are
kept within the town limits which emits offensive odors or becomes dangerous or
prejudicial to the public health, safety, moral, comfort, and welfare, and all
such acts or omissions are hereby declared NUISANCES. The health officer of the
town, or county, or state, working in conjunction with the town, shall have the
power to abate the NUISANCES.
      (C)   Any building or part thereof within the town which is in danger of
falling or otherwise in such condition as to be unsafe for persons passing
under or near the same, or residing adjacent or near thereto, or which
endangers any property contiguous or near thereto.
      (D)   Any scaffolding or other structure used in the erection or repair
of any building which is not secure and sufficiently wide to ensure the safety
of persons working thereon or passing thereunder.
      (E)   Any privy, cesspool, or water closet kept or permitted to remain in
such a condition as to become offensive or dangerous to any person in the
vicinity thereof.
      (F)   The establishment or maintenance of any cemetery or burial lot
within the town limits; provided however, that this division (F) shall not
apply to any cemetery or burial lot heretofore established by any person or to
the establishment of any cemetery by the town.
      (G)   The deposit of any rubbish, ashes, garbage, dead animals, or other
filth, refuse, or offensive matter upon any street, sidewalk, alley, public
ground, watercourse, railway right-of-way, or upon private property of any
person within the town.
      (H)   Permitting dense smoke or dust or soot to issue or emit from any
machine or building within the town limits.
      (I)   Any obstructions or encroachment upon public highways, private
ways, or streets and alleys.
      (J)   Any weeds, such as jimson, burdock, ragweed, thistle, cocklebur,
sweet clover, or other weeds or tall grasses, growing on any lot, block, or
parcel of land situated along any street in the town or in the space between
the curb line and the sidewalk line of any street or alley.
      (K)   Maintaining, storing, or otherwise keeping property of any kind or
nature in such physical condition as to be considered within the commonly
accepted term JUNK upon public or private property in such a manner as to be
exposed to the view of any neighbor or other person within the immediate
vicinity of the place where said JUNK is stored or maintained. The term JUNK
shall include, but not exclusively, motor or other vehicles from which parts
have been removed, or motor or other vehicles brought upon such property with
the intent to remove parts for the repair of other vehicles, whether motorized
or not, which are in such a state of disrepair as to be unfit for the purposes
for which they were originally intended.
      (L)   Parking or storing junk vehicles or disabled vehicles, including
trailers and parts for vehicles or trailers unless so licensed to do same.
Accumulations of inoperative junked or abandoned vehicles or machinery or any
other material which may create an UNATTRACTIVE NUISANCE or danger to children,
or to the public at large, whether on public or private property, unless same
is kept within a building or garage or completely fenced away from public view,
and protected from the possibility of access by children.
      (M)   Parking, maintaining, or storing commercial type vehicle, which may
create an unattractive nuisance or danger to children, or to the public at
large on public or private property in areas zoned as residential shall be
illegal. COMMERCIAL TYPE VEHICLE shall be defined as any oversized vehicle such
as semi-tractor trailer, charter/school bus, tractor backhoe/end loader, or
vehicle used in a commercial setting as to haul, load, or transport goods.
      (N)   The maintenance, storing, or otherwise keeping of rubber tires in
such a manner as the said tires will retain or allow for the pooling of water,
shall be deemed a PUBLIC NUISANCE and considered unlawful.
      (O)   The maintaining, storing, or keeping of firewood in a manner, which
will allow the nesting or shelter of rodents or other undesirable animals,
shall be deemed a NUISANCE, and considered unlawful.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.03 OTHER NUISANCES.
   All nuisances not defined by or provided for in this subchapter, but which
are declared to be nuisances by the statutes of the state, are hereby declared
to be nuisances and may be proceeded against under the provisions of this
subchapter.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008)
Sec. 91.04 ABATEMENT OF PUBLIC NUISANCES.
   Whenever a complaint is made to the President of the Board of Trustees that
a public nuisance exists or has existed within the town limits, the President
or official designee shall promptly inspect or cause to be inspected the
premises and shall make a written report of the findings to the Board of
Trustees. Whenever practicable, the inspecting officer may, but need not, cause
photographs to be made of the premises and may file the same in the office of
the Town Clerk so as to evidence the nature and character of the nuisance.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008)
Sec. 91.05 ABANDONED MOTOR VEHICLES.
   (1)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      ABANDONED VEHICLE. Any vehicle in a state of disrepair rendering the
vehicle incapable of being driven its condition, or any vehicle that has not
been moved for seven consecutive days or more and is apparently deserted. Any
vehicle that is not currently licensed or registered or which does not display
the same, or which is in a state of disrepair or is incapable of being driven
in its condition, is an ABANDONED VEHICLE. A NUISANCE VEHICLE is defined below
as an ABANDONED VEHICLE.
      ANTIQUE VEHICLE. Any motor vehicle or other vehicle 25 years or age or
older.
      HIGHWAY. Any street, alley, or public way within the limits of the town.
      NUISANCE VEHICLE. Any motor vehicle which is otherwise or elsewhere
defined in this chapter as a public nuisance affecting health, a public
nuisance offending morals and decency, a public nuisance affecting peace and
safety, or otherwise in this chapter defined as a public nuisance.
   (2)   Abandoning vehicle; maintaining nuisance vehicle. The abandonment of a
motor vehicle or other vehicle, or keeping or possessing of a nuisance vehicle,
is unlawful and subject to penalties as set forth herein.
      (A)   The abandonment of a motor vehicle or the keeping or possessing of
a nuisance vehicle, or any part thereof, on any highway in the town limits is
unlawful and is subject to the penalties set forth herein.
      (B)   The maintenance, keeping, or possessing of a nuisance vehicle or
other vehicle, or any part thereof, on public or private property, other than a
highway, in view of the general public, anywhere in the corporate town limits
is unlawful. A motor vehicle or other vehicle, or any part thereof, which is
defined as a nuisance vehicle, and which is on private property, may be
authorized for removal by or upon the order of the President of the Board of
Trustees as set forth below.
      (C)   The maintenance, keeping, or possessing of an abandoned vehicle or
other vehicle, or any part thereof, on public or private property, other than a
highway, in view of the general public, anywhere within the town limits is
unlawful. A motor vehicle or other vehicle, or any part thereof, which is an
abandoned vehicle, and which is on private property, may be authorized for
removal by or upon the order of the President of the Board of Trustees as set
forth in division (4) below.
      (D)   The maintenance, keeping, or possessing of a commercial type
vehicle on public or private property in areas zoned as residential shall be
unlawful.
   (3)   Notice to town. It shall be the duty of any person or company which
comes into the possession, temporary or otherwise, of any abandoned, lost,
stolen, or unclaimed motor vehicle or a nuisance vehicle within the corporate
limits of the town, to immediately notify the President of the Board of
Trustees. Upon receipt of such notification, the Board President shall
authorize a towing service to remove and take possession of the abandoned,
lost, stolen, or unclaimed motor vehicle or nuisance vehicle. The towing
service shall safely keep the towed vehicle and its contents, maintain a record
of the tow until the vehicle is claimed by the owner, or any other person
legally entitled to possession thereof, or until it is otherwise disposed of as
provided in this section.
   (4)   Removal of vehicles.
      (A)   Abandoned on highway ten hours or more. When a motor vehicle or
other vehicle is abandoned on a highway in the corporate limits of the town ten
hours or more, its removal by a towing service may be authorized by order of
the President of the Board of Trustees.
      (B)   Vehicle creating traffic hazard. When abandoned, unattended,
wrecked, burned, or partially dismantled motor vehicle is creating a traffic
hazard because of its position in relation to a highway or its physical
appearance is causing the impeding of traffic, its removal from the highway by
a towing service may be authorized by order of the President of the Board of
Trustees.
      (C)   Vehicle on private property.
   (1)   When a vehicle is an abandoned vehicle or a nuisance vehicle on
private property within the town limits, the President of the Board of
Trustees, or authorized Town Board of Trustees members or authorized employee
of the town, shall notify the owners or possessors of the said vehicles by
mailing, general delivery, to the address at which the vehicle is located, a
notice advising that the vehicle is considered an abandoned vehicle or a
nuisance vehicle as the case may be, and that the same should be removed within
a seven-day period of time and that if same is not done, that vehicle will be
towed, held for seven days, then sold as abandoned property with the proceeds
going to pay the towing, storage, and the balance to the Town Treasury. In
addition, the said town designee may cause a sticker to be affixed to the
windshield or window or other part of the vehicle, which sticker shall give
similar notice and shall bear the date upon which same is posted. If said
vehicle has not been, within the seven- day period, removed, placed in a
building, or fenced as to remove same from public view and possible access, or
otherwise cease being an abandoned vehicle or nuisance vehicle, then in such
event, the town or its designees or agents or towing services designated by the
town may come upon that private or public property for purposes of towing
vehicle, and may seek aid of law enforcement personnel if same should be
necessary or convenient, and may tow and remove said vehicle and give
possession of same to a towing service or junk or scrap dealer. The towing
service or junk or scrap dealer shall have a lien for the cost of his or her
towing and for the cost of storing the vehicle. If, at the end of seven days
after towing, the lawful owner has not claimed possession of the vehicle and
paid all towing and storage charges therefor, the said vehicle may be sold as
junk or scrap, as abandoned property, and the failure to claim ownership of
said vehicle within the period of time set forth in this division (4)(C) shall
be deemed conclusive evidence that the vehicle has been abandoned.
   (2)   Neither the town, any towing service, nor junk dealer shall have any
obligation or duty to make a search for the registered owner or other legally
entitled owner of said vehicle unless the same shall have an apparent value in
excess of $1,000, in which event the President of the Board of Trustees shall
request the County Sheriff’s Department to make a search through the Secretary
of State and the State Police records to determine whether the owner of the
vehicle can be readily ascertained. In the event such search reveals the owner,
a notice shall be mailed to the said owner by general delivery mail to the
address revealed, notifying the owner that the vehicle has been determined to
be an abandoned or nuisance vehicle, that said vehicle has been towed, and that
same will be sold for scrap on a date certain, not less than seven days from
the said letter, and said notice shall further notify the owner that he or she
may claim said vehicle by tendering all costs of storage and towing, provided
proof of ownership is established.
   (5)   Disposition of sale proceeds. Any sale proceeds of vehicles in excess
of the towing and storage charges therefor shall be deposited in the Town
Treasury.
   (6)   Liability of officers. Any town officer, towing service owner,
operator, or employee shall not be held to answer or be liable for damages in
any action brought by the registered owner, former registered owner, or his or
her legal representative, or any other person legally entitled to the
possession of a motor vehicle or the vehicle when the vehicle was processed and
sold or disposed of, as provided by this section.
   (7)   Variance. Variances for professional/commercial equipment used on a
daily basis and not held with the intent to store said equipment in a
residential area may be obtained through the Municipal Clerk’s office. This
variance will include all provisions for maintaining a commercial vehicle
within a residential area. The requestor of this variance shall agree to abide
by the provisions set forth by the Board of Trustees in order to maintain a
safe and healthy environment.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.06 DESTRUCTION OF WEEDS.
   (1)   It shall be unlawful for anyone to permit any weeds, grass, or other
plants as defined in this code to grow to a height exceeding eight inches
anywhere in the town. Such a code violation shall be deemed a nuisance.
   (2)   The owner of any premises upon which weeds or tall grasses, as defined
in this code, may be found shall be required to destroy the weeds or pay for
their destruction. The Board of Trustees, or any law enforcement officer or
Health Officer, may give the owner or occupant of such premises a notice in
writing of the existence of such nuisance, and if the weeds on such premises
are not destroyed or tall grasses cut to reasonable length by the owner or
occupant within five days after the delivery of this notice, the weeds shall be
destroyed or grasses cut by the town at the expense of the owner, and expense
shall be paid to the town by the occupant or owner of the property. This
expense shall be considered a municipal service and included in the municipal
service fee. Any unpaid municipal service bills may cause the discontinuation
of the municipal service agreement.
   (3)   For the purpose of this section, the following definition applies
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
WEEDS. Shall include, but not be limited to: burdock, rag weed, wild carrot,
poison ivy, wild mustard, rough pig weed, jimson, blue vervain, common milk
weed, thistle, cocklebur, lambsquarter, wild lettuce, curled dock, smartweeds
(all varieties), poison hemlock, wild hemp, and Johnson grass, including all
other NOXIOUS WEEDS as defined by the statutes of the state.
   (4)   If the owner or occupant does not abate the nuisance within five days,
the town shall proceed to abate the nuisance and bill the owner as a municipal
service charge at a rate set forth by the Board of Trustees.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.07 BURNING OF YARD WASTE.
   (1)   For the purpose of this section, the following definition applies
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      YARD WASTE. Dry leaves, sticks, wood, and dry grass/weeds. No
construction materials will be considered as YARD WASTE or allowed.
   (2)   (A)   All fires must be contained within a three to four foot
diameter. No fires/burning will be allowed to emit dense smoke.
      (B)   The burning of landscape waste will be allowed only between sunrise
and sunset. Fires must be extinguished by dusk.
      (C)   Recreational fires will be allowed in safe areas and will be
extinguished by 1:00 a.m.
      (D)   Whenever burning, citizens are asked to take into consideration
their surroundings, and the wind conditions, be a good citizen and courteous to
their neighbor.
      (E)   All noncompliant fires/burning will be extinguished at the resident
or property owner’s expense.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.08 BUILDINGS AND STRUCTURES.
   (1)   Definition. All buildings or structures which have any or all of the
following defects shall be deemed DANGEROUS AND UNSAFE:
      (A)   Those whose interior walls or other vertical structural members
list, lean, or buckle to such an extent that a plumb line passing through the
center of gravity falls outside of the middle third of its base;
      (B)   Those which, exclusive of the foundation, show 31% or more of
damage or deterioration of the supporting members, or 50% of damage or
deterioration of the nonsupporting enclosing or outside walls or covering;
      (C)   Those which have been damaged by fire, wind, or other causes so far
as to become dangerous to life, safety, morals, or the general health and
welfare of the occupants or the citizens of the community;
      (D)   Those which have become or are so dilapidated, decayed, unsafe,
unsanitary, or which so utterly fail to provide the necessities essential to
decent living that they are unfit for human habitation or likely to cause
sickness or disease, so as to cause injury to health, morals, safety, or
general welfare of those living therein;
      (E)   Those having inadequate facilities for egress in the case of fire
or panic or those having insufficient stairways, elevators, fire escapes, or
other means of access;
      (F)   Those which have parts thereof which are so attached that they may
fall, injuring members of the public or the property;
      (G)   Those having light, air, and sanitation facilities which are
inadequate to protect the health, morals, safety, or general welfare of human
beings who live or may live therein;
      (H)   Those buildings existing in violation of any provisions of the
building code of this community or any provision of the fire prevention code,
or any other ordinances of the town;
      (I)   Those vacant buildings with unguarded openings shall be deemed to
constitute a fire hazard and to be unsafe within the provisions of this code;
and/or
      (J)   Those buildings which are incomplete or abandoned.
   (2)   Standards for repair, vacation, or demolition.
      (A)   The following standards shall be followed in substance by an
approved building inspector in ordering repair, vacation, or demolition.
         (1)   If the dangerous and unsafe building is in such condition as to
make it dangerous to the health, safety, morals, or general welfare of its
occupants, it shall be ordered to be vacated.
         (2)   If the dangerous and unsafe building can reasonably be repaired
so that it will no longer exist in violation of the terms of this code, it
shall be ordered repaired.
         (3)   In any case where a dangerous and unsafe building is 50% damaged
or decayed or deteriorated from its original value or structure, it shall be
demolished, and in all cases where a building cannot be repaired so that it
will no longer exist in violation of the terms of this code, it shall be
demolished. In all cases where a dangerous and unsafe building is a fire hazard
existing or erected in violation of the terms of this code, or any ordinance of
the community or statute of the state, it shall be demolished.
      (B)   All dangerous and unsafe buildings within the terms of this section
are hereby declared to be public nuisance and shall be repaired, vacated, or
demolished as provided in this division (2).
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.09 BARKING DOGS; ANIMAL NOISE.
   (1)   It shall be unlawful for any person to allow a dog or other animals to
make loud or disturbing noises for extended periods of time. Any dog or animal
that barks or makes disturbing noises continuously or intermittently for more
than 30 minutes shall be deemed a nuisance.
   (2)   It shall also be unlawful to allow animals to defecate on public
properties within the town limits. Animal owners shall be responsible to
maintain animals on a leash at all times. Any animal waste shall not be left on
public property or citizen’s private property.
   (3)   Official complaints identifying the nuisance must be registered with
the Municipal Clerk and signed by the petitioner.
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.10 UNWHOLESOME FOOD.
   It shall be unlawful to sell or offer for sale any unwholesome or polluted
food of any kind in the town.
(Ord. 17.3, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.11 SANITARY REGULATIONS.
   All premises used for the sale or storage of food or drink intended for
human consumption shall be kept in a clean and sanitary condition under the
guidelines of the State Department of Public Health Safety and Sanitation Code.
(Ord. 17.3, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.12 WATERCOURSES.
   It shall be unlawful and a nuisance for any person to obstruct or pollute
any watercourse or source of water supply in the town.
(Ord. 17.3, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.13 SPITTING.
   It shall be unlawful to spit or expectorate on any public sidewalk or other
public place, or on the floor or wall of any store, theater, hall, public
vehicle, or other place frequented by the public or to which the public is
invited.
(Ord. 17.3, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.14 DENSE SMOKE.
   It shall be unlawful to cause or permit the emission of dense smoke.
(Ord. 17.3, passed 2-19-2008) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
PLANTS AND WEEDS
Sec. 91.25 WEEDS DECLARED A NUISANCE.
   Any weeds, such as jimson, burdock, giant ragweed, common ragweed,
cocklebur, blue vervain, common milkweed, wild carrots, poison ivy, wild
mustard, rust pigweed, lambsquarter, wild lettuce, curled dock, smart weed (all
varieties), poison hemlock, and wild hemp, or other weeds of a like kind, found
growing in any lot or tract of land in the town are hereby declared to be a
nuisance, and it shall be unlawful to permit any such weeds to grow or remain
in any such place.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.1) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.26 HEIGHT.
   It shall be unlawful for anyone to permit any weeds, grass, or plants, other
than trees, bushes, flowers, or other ornamental plants to grow to a height
exceeding eight inches anywhere in the town. Any such plants or weeds exceeding
such height are hereby declared to be a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.2) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.27 BARBERRY BUSHES.
   It shall be a nuisance and unlawful to plant or permit the growth of the
bush of the species of tall, common, or European barberry, further known as
Barberis Vulgaris, or its horticultural varieties, within the town.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.3) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.28 REMOVAL, NOTICE.
   It shall be the duty of the Town Police to serve or cause to be served a
notice upon the owner or occupant of any premises on which weeds or plants are
permitted to grow in violation of the provisions of this subchapter and to
demand the abatement of the nuisance within ten days.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.4) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.29 ABATEMENT.
   If the person so served does not abate the nuisance within ten days, the
town may proceed to abate such nuisance, keeping an account of the expense of
the abatement, and such expense shall be charged to and paid by such owner or
occupant.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.5)
Sec. 91.30 LIEN.
   Charges for such weed removal shall be a lien upon the premises. Whenever a
bill for such charges remains unpaid for 60 days after it has been rendered,
the Clerk may file with the Recorder of Deeds a statement of lien claim. This
statement shall contain a legal description of the premises, the expenses and
costs incurred and the date the weeds were cut, and a notice that the town
claims a lien for this amount. Notice of such lien claim shall be mailed to the
owner of the premises if his or her address is known. Provided, however, that
failure of the Clerk to record such lien claim or to mail such notice, or the
failure of the owner to receive such notice, shall not affect the right to
foreclose the line for such charges as provided in Sec.
91.31.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.6)
Sec. 91.31 FORECLOSURE OF LIEN.
   Property subject to a lien for unpaid weed cutting charges shall be sold for
nonpayment of the same and the proceeds of such sale shall be applied to pay
the charges after deducting costs, as is the case in the foreclosure of
statutory liens. Such foreclosure shall be in equity in the name of the town.
The Town Attorney is hereby authorized and directed to institute such
proceedings, in the name of the town, in any court having jurisdiction over
such matter, against any property for which such bill has remained unpaid 60
days after it has been rendered.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.7)
GRAFFITI
Sec. 91.45 INTENT AND PURPOSE.
   In order to reduce blight and deterioration, and to protect the public
health and safety, within the town, it is the purpose and intent of this
subchapter to provide a procedure for the removal of graffiti from surfaces on
any public or private property when such graffiti is visible from a public
street, public alley, or other public right-of-way of the town.
(Ord. 466, passed 9-14-2017)
Sec. 91.46 DEFINITION; PROHIBITION.
   (1)   Definition. For the purpose of this subchapter, the following
definition applies unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      GRAFFITI.
      (1)   Any unauthorized inscription, word, figure, picture, or design that
is sprayed, marked, posted, pasted, or otherwise affixed, drawn, or painted on
any surface of public or private property.
      (2)   GRAFFITI shall not be defined as a sign or art work lawfully
erected and maintained as required by other ordinances.
   (2)   Prohibition.
      (A)   It is unlawful for any person to place graffiti upon any public or
private property within the town.
      (B)   It is unlawful for any person to maintain graffiti that has been
placed upon, or allow graffiti to remain upon, any surface within that person’s
control, possession, or ownership when the graffiti is visible from a public
street, public alley, other public right-of-way, and other public property.
      (C)   Graffiti prohibited under this subchapter is hereby declared a
public nuisance.
(Ord. 466, passed 9-14-2017) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.47 ABATEMENT PROCEDURE.
   Graffiti nuisances shall be proceeded against as follows.
   (1)   Upon finding a graffiti nuisance on private property, the Board of
Trustees shall give notice to the property owner as provided in this
subchapter. If the condition has not been abated within the time required, the
town is authorized to abate the nuisance.
   (2)   The property owner shall be responsible for the removal of graffiti.
   (3)   If the owner or person in control of such property is a nonresident of
the town and has no business address in the town, such notice shall be posted
and deemed served at the end of such.
   (4)   All graffiti nuisances shall be abated within 72 hours after
notification.
   (5)   All graffiti nuisances abated by the town shall be done at the expense
of the property owner. All expenses incurred shall be charged on the municipal
service bill to such property.
(Ord. 466, passed 9-14-2017) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.48 RIGHT OF ENTRY.
   Any person employed, or contracted with, for the abatement of a nuisance as
provided for in this subchapter, and any agent or employee of such person,
shall have the right of entry for the abatement purpose upon any premises. Any
interference with such entry shall be considered a violation of this
subchapter.
(Ord. 466, passed 9-14-2017) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
DANGEROUS BUILDINGS
Sec. 91.60 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition applies unless
the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      DANGEROUS BUILDINGS.
      (A)   Any building, shed, fence, or other human-made structure which is
dangerous to the public health because of its condition, and which may cause or
aid in the spread of disease, or injury to the health of the occupants of it or
neighboring structures.
      (B)   Any building, shed, fence, or other human-made structure which,
because of faulty construction, age, lack of proper repair, or any other cause,
is especially liable to fire and constitutes or creates a fire hazard.
      (C)   Any building, shed, fence, or other human-made structure which, by
reason of faulty construction or other cause, is liable to cause injury or
damage by collapsing or by a collapse or fall of any part of such structure.
      (D)   Any building, shed, fence, or other human-made structure which,
because of its condition or because of lack of doors or windows, is available
to and frequented by malefactors or disorderly persons who are not lawful
occupants of such structure. Any such DANGEROUS BUILDING in the town is hereby
declared to be a nuisance.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.2.1)
Sec. 91.61 PROHIBITION.
   It shall be unlawful to maintain or permit the existence of any dangerous
building in the town, and it shall be unlawful for the owner, occupant, or
person in custody of any dangerous building to permit the same to remain in a
dangerous condition, or to occupy such building or permit the same to be
occupied while it is or remains in a dangerous condition.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.2.2) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.62 ABATEMENT.
   (1)   Whenever the Town Police shall be of the opinion that any building or
structure in the town is a dangerous building, he or she shall file a written
statement to this effect with the Town Clerk. The Clerk shall thereupon cause
written notice to be served upon the owner thereof, and upon the occupant
thereof, if any, by registered mail or by personal service. Such notice shall
state that the building has been declared to be in a dangerous condition, and
that such dangerous condition must be removed or remedied by repairing or
altering the building or by demolishing it, and that the condition must be
remedied at once.
   (2)   Such notice may be in the following form:
 
To ___________ (owner-occupant of such premises) or the premises known and
described as ________________________________________,
You are hereby notified that (describe building) on the premises above-mentioned
has been condemned as a nuisance and a dangerous building after inspection by the
Town Police’s causes for this decision are (here insert facts as to the dangerous
condition).
You must remedy this condition or demolish the building immediately, or the town
will proceed to do so.
 
   (3)   If the person receiving the notice has not complied therewith or taken
an appeal from the determination of the officer or employee finding that a
dangerous building exists, within ten days from the time when this notice is
served upon such person by personal service or registered mail, the Town Police
may, upon orders of the Mayor and Board of Trustees, proceed to remedy the
condition or demolish the dangerous building.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.2.3)
Sec. 91.63 FIRE LIMITS.
   (1) (A)Any frame building or structure within the fire limits of the town
are hereinbefore prescribed by ordinance, which has or may be damaged by fire,
decay, or other causes to the extent of 50% of its value, shall be torn down
and removed, or rebuilt with nonflammable walls.
      (B)   Upon written notice by the Town Police to the effect that such
building has been so damaged, filed with the Clerk, said Clerk shall notify the
Mayor and Board of Trustees of the receipt of such notice. The Mayor and Board
of Trustees shall then appoint three persons to determine whether or not such
building or structure has been damaged to the extent of 50% of its value.
      (C)   A copy of the notice of the appointment of this board of three
persons to determine the damage shall be served upon the owner of the premises
by personal service or by registered mail at his or her last known address.
   (2)   Such notice shall be substantially the following form:
 
To ___________, you are hereby notified that the Mayor and Board of Trustees
has determined that the building owned by you at _________ located within the
fire limits of the town has been damaged by fire, decay, or otherwise to the
extent of 50% of its value and that a board of three persons has been appointed
to verify this finding, which board will hold its first meeting in the Town
Hall on the ___ day of _____ at the hour of _____ o’clock, at which time it
will determine whether or not this finding is correct.
If this finding is verified by the board, you must tear down and remove the
said building, or rebuild it with nonflammable walls.
 
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.2.4) Penalty, see Sec.
91.99
Sec. 91.99 PENALTY.
   (1)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to Sec. 10.99 of this code of
ordinances.
   (2)   (A)   (1)   Owners of animals found to be in violation of Sec.
91.09 shall be given an oral warning on the first offense.
         (2)   Owners of animals found to be in violation of Sec.
91.09 shall be given a written warning on the second offense.
         (3)   Owners of animals found to be in violation of Sec.
91.09 shall be fined for the third offense or more.
         (4)   Fines shall be determined by division (2)(B) below.
      (B)   Violations or multiple violations of Sec.
91.01 to
91.09 may result in fines of not less than $50 and not more than $500.
   (3)   Any person violating any of the provisions of Sec.
91.25 to
91.31 shall be fined in accordance with Sec.
10.99, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on each day during or
on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.4.8)
   (4)   (A)   Any person or persons found to be committing the act of causing
graffiti on municipal or private property in violation of Sec.
91.45 to
91.48 shall be prosecuted for a criminal offense.
      (B)   Violations of multiple violations of Sec.
91.45 to
91.48 may result in fines of not less than $50 and not more than $500.
   (5)   Any person violating any provisions of Sec.
91.60 to
91.63, or permitting any dangerous building, or any building or structure to
remain in a dangerous condition, or to remain in the fire limits, without
remodeling as provided for in Sec.
91.62, after it has been damaged to the extent of 50% of its value, shall be
fined not less than $25 nor more than $500 for each offense, and a separate
offense shall be deemed committed on each day during or on which a violation
occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, Sec. 18.2.5)
(Ord. 17.2, passed 2-19-2008; Ord. 466, passed 9-14-2017)
CHAPTER 92: PARKS AND RECREATION
Section
   
92.01   George Walters Memorial Park
   
92.02   Town Community Building
   
92.03   Parks rules and regulations
 
   
92.99   Penalty
Sec. 92.01 GEORGE WALTERS MEMORIAL PARK.
   The Mayor and Board of Trustees have created the George Walters Memorial
Park.
(Res. 474, passed 9-27-2018)
Sec. 92.02 TOWN COMMUNITY BUILDING.
   The town building at 326 East Locust Street be used as a community center
and be regulated by the Board of Trustees.
(Ord. 443, passed 3-24-2016)
Sec. 92.03 PARKS RULES AND REGULATIONS.
   (1)   Park rules and regulations:
      (A)   No profanity or music containing profanity;
      (B)   No alcohol or controlled substance;
      (C)   No littering;
      (D)   No open fires;
      (E)   No loud music;
      (F)   No firearms;
      (G)   No bullying or fighting;
      (H)   No fireworks;
      (I)   No glass containers;
      (J)   No sitting on park tabletops;
      (K)   No bicycles on walks or courts;
      (L)   All pets must be on a leash;
      (M)   All pet owners must clean up after pets;
      (N)   Autos and golf carts in designated parking areas only;
      (O)   Children under eight years of age must be accompanied by an adult;
      (P)   No smoking or use of tobacco under pavilion or within 15 feet of
playground equipment;
      (Q)   Damaging public property will result in legal action;
      (R)   No swimming; and
      (S)   No boating.
   (2)   Park hours: 6:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m.
   (3)   Special events can be scheduled through the Town Clerk.
(Ord. 475, passed 9-27-2018) Penalty, see Sec.
92.99
Sec. 92.99 PENALTY.
   (1)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to Sec.
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (2)   Failure to comply with the park rules and regulations in Sec.
92.03 will result in penalty fines and no trespass warrants.
(Ord. 475, passed 9-27-2018)
TITLE XI: BUSINESS REGULATIONS
   Chapter

CHAPTER 110: GENERAL PERMIT AND LICENSING REQUIREMENTS
Section
   
110.01   Applications
   
110.02   Person subject to license
   
110.03   Forms
   
110.04   Signatures
   
110.05   License year
   
110.06   Building and premises
   
110.07   Change of location
   
110.08   Nuisances
   
110.09   Inspections
   
110.10   Revocation of license
   
110.11   Investigations
   
110.12   Fees
   
110.13   Posting license
 
   
110.99   Penalty
Sec. 110.01 APPLICATIONS.
   Applications for all licenses and permits required by ordinance shall be
made in writing to the Town Clerk, in the absence of provision to the contrary.
Each application shall state the name of the applicant, the permit or license
desired, the location to be used, if any, the time covered, and the fee to be
paid, and each application shall contain such additional information as may be
needed for the proper guidance of the town officials in the issuing of the
permit or license applied for.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.1)
Sec. 110.02 PERSON SUBJECT TO LICENSE.
   Whenever in this code a license is required for the maintenance, operation,
or conduct of any business or establishment, or for doing business or engaging
in any activity or occupation, any person or corporation shall be subject to
the requirement if, by himself or herself, or through an agent, employee, or
partner, he or she holds himself or herself forth as being engaged in the
business or occupation or solicits patronage therefor, actively or passively,
or performs or attempts to perform any part of any such business or occupation
in the town.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.2) Penalty, see Sec.
110.99
Sec. 110.03 FORMS.
   Forms for all licenses and permits, and applications therefor, shall be
prepared and kept on file by the Town Clerk.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.3)
Sec. 110.04 SIGNATURES.
   Each license or permit shall bear the signatures of the Mayor and the Clerk,
in the absence of any provision to the contrary.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.4)
Sec. 110.05 LICENSE YEAR.
   The license year for the town shall begin on May 1 and end on April 30 of
each year.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.5)
Sec. 110.06 BUILDING AND PREMISES.
   No license shall be issued for the conduct of any business, and no permit
shall be issued for any thing or act, if the premises and building to be used
for the purpose do not fully comply with the ordinance requirements of the
town. No such license or permit shall be issued for the conduct of any business
or performance of any act which would involve a violation of the zoning
ordinance of the town.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.6)
Sec. 110.07 CHANGE OF LOCATION.
   The location of any licensed business or occupation, or of any permitted
act, may be changed; provided, that the building and zoning requirements of the
town are complied with.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.7)
Sec. 110.08 NUISANCES.
   No business, licensed or not, shall be so conducted or operated as to amount
to a nuisance in fact.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.8) Penalty, see Sec.
110.99
Sec. 110.09 INSPECTIONS.
   Whenever inspections of the premises which are used for or in connection
with the operation of a licensed business or occupation are provided for or
required by ordinance, or are reasonably necessary to secure compliance with
any ordinance provision or to detect violations thereof, it shall be the duty
of the licensee, or the person in charge of the premises to be inspected, to
admit thereto for the purpose of making the inspection any officer or employee
of the town who is authorized or directed to make such inspection at any
reasonable time that admission is requested. Whenever an analysis of any
commodity or material is reasonably necessary to secure conformance with any
ordinance provision or to detect violations thereof, it shall be the duty of
the licensee of the municipality whose business is governed by such provision
to give to any authorized officer or employee of the town requesting the same
sufficient samples of such material or commodity for such analysis upon
request. In addition to any other penalty which may be provided, the Town Mayor
may revoke the license of any licensed proprietor of any licensed business in
the town who refused to permit any such officer or employee who is authorized
to make such inspection or take such samples to make the inspection, or take an
employee while in the performance of his or her duty in making such inspection.
Provided, that a license shall not be revoked for such cause unless written
demand is made upon the licensee or person in charge of the premises, in the
name of the town, stating that such inspection or sample is desired at the time
it is sought to make the inspection or obtain the sample.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.9) Penalty, see Sec.
110.99
Sec. 110.10 REVOCATION OF LICENSE.
   Any license or permit for a limited time may be revoked by the Town Mayor at
any time during the life of such license or permit for any violation by the
licensee or permitted of the ordinance provisions relating to the license or
permit, the subject matter of the license or permit, or to the premises
occupied. Such revocation may be in addition to any fine imposed.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.10)
Sec. 110.11 INVESTIGATIONS.
   Upon the receipt of an application for a license or permit where ordinances
of the town necessitate inspection or investigation before the issuance of such
permit or license, the Town Clerk shall refer such application to the officer
for making such investigation within 48 hours of the time of such receipt. The
officer in charge of making the investigation or inspection shall make a report
thereon, favorable or otherwise, within ten days after receiving the
application or a copy thereof.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.11)
Sec. 110.12 FEES.
   In the absence of provision to the contrary, all fees and charges for
licenses or permits shall be paid in advance at the time application therefore
is made to the Town Clerk. When an applicant has not engaged in the business
until after the expiration of a part of the current license year, the license
fee shall be prorated by quarters and the fees paid for each quarter or
fraction thereof during which the business has been or will be conducted.
Except as otherwise provided, all license fees shall become a part of the
Corporate Fund.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.12)
Sec. 110.13 POSTING LICENSE.
   It shall be the duty of any person conducting a licensed business in the
town to keep his or her license posted in a prominent place on the premises
used for such business at all times.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.13) Penalty, see Sec.
110.99
Sec. 110.99 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any provision of this chapter shall be fined in
accordance with Sec. 10.99 for each offense, and separate offense shall be
deemed committed on each day during or on which a violation occurs or
continues.
(Prior Code, Sec. 11.14)
CHAPTER 111: PEDDLERS, SOLICITORS, AND TRANSIENT MERCHANTS
Section
Peddlers
   
111.01   Definition
   
111.02   License required
   
111.03   License prerequisites
   
111.04   License transferability
   
111.05   License revocation
   
111.06   Marking of vehicles
   
111.07   Calling attention to sales by calling out, blowing horns
   
111.08   Exemptions
Solicitors
   
111.20   Requirements
   
111.21   Exemptions
   
111.22   Permits
Transient Merchants
   
111.35   Definition
   
111.36   Compliance
   
111.37   Exemptions
   
111.38   License required; application
   
111.39   License; issuance and fees
   
111.40   License revocation
 
   
111.99   Penalty
PEDDLERS
Sec. 111.01 DEFINITION.
For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition applies unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
PEDDLER. Every person who shall sell or offer for sale, barter, or exchange, at
retail, any goods, wares, or merchandise, except milk or cream, traveling from
place to place in, along, and upon the streets, avenues, alleys, or public
places of the town, going from place to place in the town, whether to regular
customers or not.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.1)
Sec. 111.02 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person shall engage in business as a peddler without first obtaining a
license so to do.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.2) Penalty, see Sec.
111.99
Sec. 111.03 LICENSE PREREQUISITES.
   The Mayor shall grant licenses required in Sec.
111.02 upon:
   (1)   Registration by the applicant with the Town Clerk;
   (2)   Certification by the Town Clerk to the Mayor that the applicant is of
good moral character; and
   (3)   Payment to the Town Clerk of an annual fee of $25.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.3)
Sec. 111.04 LICENSE TRANSFERABILITY.
   The license required by this subchapter shall not be transferable without
the consent of the Mayor.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.4) Penalty, see Sec.
111.99
Sec. 111.05 LICENSE REVOCATION.
   In addition to any fine, all licenses that may have been issued to any
person under this subchapter may be revoked by the Mayor upon conviction for a
violation of any of the provisions of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.5)
Sec. 111.06 MARKING OF VEHICLES.
   Every wagon, cart, or other vehicle used by a person licensed under this
subchapter shall be conspicuously and permanently marked with the name of such
person and with the number of his or her license.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.6) Penalty, see Sec.
111.99
Sec. 111.07 CALLING ATTENTION TO SALES BY CALLING OUT, BLOWING HORNS.
   No person, whether he or she has a peddler’s license or not, in going along
the streets, alleys, highways, or public places of the town, shall call out,
cry, or by any device make any noise or blow any horn to call attention to the
sales of any goods, wares, merchandise, or any article or thing whatsoever.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.7) Penalty, see Sec.
111.99
Sec. 111.08 EXEMPTIONS.
   This subchapter shall not be so construed as to prevent any farmer, fruit or
vine grower and gardener from the right to sell the product of his or her farm,
orchard, vineyard, and garden with the town, in any quantity he or she may
think proper, without obtaining a license therefor; nor shall the same be so
construed as to make it a penal offense for children under the age of 12 years
to peddle apples or other fruit; provided, that they do not occupy a stand; nor
shall it be so construed as to apply to the peddling of newspapers.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.8)
SOLICITORS
Sec. 111.20 REQUIREMENTS.
   (1)   To protect and maintain the public health, safety, and general
welfare, door-to-door solicitation and canvassing is regulated by the town. The
town prohibits any person or organization from conducting solicitations and
canvassing of residences without a permit. Permits shall be obtained from the
Town Clerk during regular business hours. It shall be a violation of this
subchapter to engage in solicitation and canvassing without a permit.
   (2)   Parties engaging in door-to-door solicitation must carry their permit
while they solicit door-to- door. The solicitation permit shall be presented to
citizens upon request. Copies of valid permits will be maintained by the Town
Clerk.
   (3)   No solicitation or canvassing shall be allowed before 9:00 a.m. or
after 5:00 p.m. Violations of this subchapter should be reported as quickly as
possible to the Town Hall. No solicitations or canvassing shall be allowed
where no solicitation signs are posted.
(Ord. 457, passed 2-9-2017) Penalty, see Sec. 111.99
Sec. 111.21 EXEMPTIONS.
   These regulations do not apply to: recognized youth organization, including
school sponsored clubs; canvassing conducted by political candidates for public
office, their workers, or representatives from political parties; persons who
solicit for or on behalf of religious organizations, or in pursuit of religious
beliefs and activities. A permit is not required for any solicitation or
canvassing activity sponsored or conducted by the town or its departments and
committees, nor by any government agency in the town, such as the library or
Fire Department.
(Ord. 457, passed 2-9-2017)
Sec. 111.22 PERMITS.
   All permits issued by the town shall require a copy of a picture
identification of all solicitors, name, address, and current phone number of
company or organization. A $25 permit fee must be submitted at the time of
request. Permits shall include rules of this subchapter.
(Ord. 457, passed 2-9-2017)
TRANSIENT MERCHANTS
Sec. 111.35 DEFINITION.
For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition applies unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
TRANSIENT MERCHANT. Any person engaged temporarily in the retail sale of goods,
wares, or merchandise within the town, who for the purpose of conducting such
business, rents or leases any room, building, hotel, rooming house, structure,
or lot of any kind, regardless of whether such goods, wares, or merchandise are
peddled from house to house, place to place, or sold from the room, building,
hotel, rooming house, structure, or lot.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.1)
Sec. 111.36 COMPLIANCE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to engage in business as a transient
merchant, as defined by this subchapter, within the town until the provisions
of this subchapter have been complied with.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.2) Penalty, see Sec.
111.99
Sec. 111.37 EXEMPTIONS.
   The provisions of this subchapter shall not apply to sales made to dealers
by commercial travelers or selling agents in usual course of business.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.3)
Sec. 111.38 LICENSE REQUIRED; APPLICATION.
   Any person desiring to engage in business as a transient merchant within the
town shall make a sworn application, in writing, at least seven days before
such applicant shall be authorized to begin such business. Such application
shall state the business, the name and address of the applicant, the place
where such business is to be conducted, the kind of goods to be sold, and the
length of time for which the license is desired.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.4) Penalty, see Sec.
111.99
Sec. 111.39 LICENSE; ISSUANCE AND FEES.
   (1)   Upon the filing of the application and the approval thereof, a license
shall be issued by the Mayor to such applicant upon payment of the following
fee: $10 for one year. All such license fees must be paid in advance.
   (2)   If any such licensee desires to continue in business after the
expiration of such license, a new license must be secured in the same manner
and upon the same terms as the original license.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.5)
Sec. 111.40 LICENSE REVOCATION.
   The Mayor shall have the right to revoke the license of a transient merchant
if he or she shall be satisfied that the licensee is not responsible, or has
made false representation or statements in the application for such license.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.6)
Sec. 111.99 PENALTY.
   (1)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to Sec.
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (2)   Any person violating any of the provisions of Sec.
111.01 to
111.08 shall be fined in accordance with Sec.
10.99 for each offense, and separate offense shall be deemed committed on each
day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.1.9)
   (3)   Any person violating any of the provisions of Sec.
111.35 to
111.40 shall be fined in accordance with Sec.
10.99 for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, Sec. 14.3.7)
CHAPTER 112: AMUSEMENTS
Section
   
112.01   Application of chapter
   
112.02   Street shows
   
112.03   Order, crowding
   
112.04   Inspections
   
112.05   Exhibition of criminals
   
112.06   Riots
   
112.07   Exit lights
 
   
112.99   Penalty
Sec. 112.01 APPLICATION OF CHAPTER.
   The provisions of this chapter shall apply to all public shows, theatricals,
circuses, and other amusements in the town, whether specifically licensed in
another article of this code or not.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.1)
Sec. 112.02 STREET SHOWS.
   No permit for any circus, exhibition, show, or other amusement to be given
on any public street or sidewalk or in such a place that the only main
accommodation for the public or the audience will be in a public place, shall
be issued except on order of the Mayor and Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.2)
Sec. 112.03 ORDER, CROWDING.
   The audience of any amusement, show, or theatrical must be orderly and quiet
at all times, and it shall be unlawful for any person attending any such
amusement, show, or theatrical to create a disturbance in the audience. It
shall be unlawful to permit or gather such a crowd to witness any amusement or
show as to create a dangerous condition because of fire or other risks.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.3) Penalty, see Sec.
112.99
Sec. 112.04 INSPECTIONS.
   It shall be the duty of the Town Mayor to see that every exhibition,
amusement, theatrical, or other public show is inspected to ensure conformity
with the provisions concerning such amusements.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.4)
Sec. 112.05 EXHIBITION OF CRIMINALS.
   It shall be unlawful to exhibit any criminal or the body of any criminal or
to exhibit any person who shall be notorious because of the commission of a
crime, in any theatrical, exhibit, carnival, or other public show or place.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.5) Penalty, see Sec.
112.99
Sec. 112.06 RIOTS.
   It shall be unlawful to present in any public amusement or show of any kind
anything which tends to, or is calculated to, cause or promote a riot or
disturbance.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.6) Penalty, see Sec.
112.99
Sec. 112.07 EXIT LIGHTS.
   It shall be the duty of the owner or occupant in charge of any building or
hall used as an assembly hall in which theatricals, shows, amusements,
lectures, and other entertainments are offered, operated, or presented, to
provide and place a sign on which the word “exit” shall appear in letters at
least six inches high, over a door or other opening from such hall to every
means of egress therefrom, and a light shall be kept burning during the entire
period that the hall is open to the public and until the audience has left the
hall.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.7) Penalty, see Sec.
112.99
Sec. 112.99 PENALTY.
   Any person violating any provision of this chapter shall be fined in
accordance with Sec.
10.99 for each offense, and a separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, Sec. 12.8)
CHAPTER 113: ALCOHOL
