GENERAL PROVISIONS
Sec. 153.001 ZONING ENFORCEMENT OFFICER.
   (1)   Creation of office. There is hereby created the office of Zoning
Enforcement Officer, who shall be appointed by the Mayor, with advice and
consent of the Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, Sec. 4.3.1)
   (2)   Duties. It shall be the duty of the Zoning Enforcement Officer to
enforce all the provisions of the zoning ordinance and amendments thereto. He
or she shall perform such additional duties as may be assigned to him or her by
the Mayor and Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, Sec. 4.3.2)
   (3)   Powers. He or she shall have the powers and exercise the functions
necessary for such enforcement which are delegated to him or her by statute or
ordinance.
(Prior Code, Sec. 4.3.4)
Sec. 153.002 TITLE.
   This chapter shall be known, cited, and referred to as the “Chatsworth
Zoning Ordinance.”
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.1.1)
Sec. 153.003 INTENT AND PURPOSE.
   This chapter is adopted for the following reasons:
   (1)   To promote and protect the public health, safety, morals, comfort, and
general welfare of the people;
   (2)   To divide the town into zones or districts restricting and regulating
therein the location, erection, construction, reconstruction, alteration, and
use of buildings, structures, and land for residence, business, manufacturing,
and other specified uses;
   (3)   To protect the character and stability of the residential, business,
and manufacturing areas within the town and to promote the orderly and
beneficial development of such areas;
   (4)   To provide adequate light, air, privacy, and convenience of access to
property;
   (5)   To regulate the intensity of use of lot areas, and to determine the
area of open spaces surrounding buildings, necessary to provide adequate light
and area and to protect the public health; and
   (6)   To establish building lines and the location of buildings designed for
residential, business, and manufacturing or other uses within such areas.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.1.2)
Sec. 153.004 DEFINITIONS.
For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
ABUTTING. Having one or more common boundary lines or district lines.
ACCESSORY BUILDING OR USE.
   (A)   One which:
      (1)   Is subordinate to and serves a principal building or principal use;
      (2)   Is subordinate in area, extent, and purpose to the principal
building or principal use served; and
      (3)   Is located in the same zoning lot as the principal building or
principal use.
   (B)   An ACCESSORY USE includes, but is not limited to, the following:
      (1)   A children’s playhouse, garden house, and private greenhouse;
      (2)   A shed, garage, or building for domestic storage;
      (3)   Carports; and
      (4)   Satellite dishes, antennas, and other similar structures.
ADVERTISING STRUCTURE. Any notice or advertisement, pictorial or otherwise, and
all such structures used as an outdoor display, regardless of size and shape,
for the purpose of making anything known, the origin or place of sale of which
is not on the property where such ADVERTISING STRUCTURE is located.
AGRICULTURE. The growing, harvesting, and storing of crops, including legumes,
hay, grain, fruit, and truck or vegetable crops, floriculture, horticulture,
viticulture, mushroom growing, orchards, and forestry; farm buildings for
storing and protecting farm machinery and equipment from the elements; and farm
buildings occupied by farm owners, operators, tenants, or seasonal or year-
round hired farm workers. This definition of AGRICULTURE includes all types of
agricultural operations, but excludes therefrom animal husbandry and industrial
operations such as a grain elevator, canning, or slaughterhouse, wherein
agricultural products primarily by others are stored or processed.
AIRCRAFT. Any contrivance now known or hereafter invented for use in or
designed for navigation or flight in the air.
AIRPORT. Any area of land which issued or intended for use for the landing and
taking off of aircraft, and any appurtenant areas which are used or intended
for use for airport buildings or other airport facilities or right-of-way,
including all necessary taxiways, aircraft storage and tie-down areas, hangars,
and other necessary buildings and open spaces.
ALTERATION. Any change in size, shape, or character of a building or structure
in the use thereof.
AUTOMOBILE SERVICE STATION. Buildings and premises where gasoline, oil, grease,
batteries, tires, and automobile accessories may be supplied and dispensed at
retail.
AWNING. A roof-like cover which projects from the wall of a building and
overhangs into the yard or public or private street.
BASEMENT. The portion of a building which is partly below and partly above
grade and having at least one-half its height above grade.
BLOCK. A tract of land bounded by public streets, or by a combination of
streets and public parks, cemeteries, railroad rights-of-way, shorelines, or
waterways, the corporate limits of the town.
BUILDING. Any structure built for the support, shelter, or enclosure of
persons, animals, chattels, or movable property of any kind.
BUILDING HEIGHT. The measurement of vertical distance from the grade to the top
of the highest roof beams of a flat roof or to the mean level of the highest
gable or slope of a hip roof.
BULK. A term used to measure, assess, and regulate the impact of buildings,
structures, or land uses on other nearby buildings, structures, or land uses,
and includes the following factors:
   (A)   Size and height of buildings;
   (B)   Location of exterior walls and required onsite facilities at all
levels in relation to lot lines, public streets, or to other public buildings;
   (C)   Gross floor area of buildings in relation to lot area (floor area
ratio);
   (D)   All open spaces allocated to buildings;
   (E)   Amount of lot area provided per dwelling unit; and
   (F)   Type, amount, and location of landscaping and site screening used to
shield or reduce the impact of land uses on surrounding property.
CARPORT. An automobile shelter, usually formed by extension of the roof from
the side of a building which may be open on the sides.
CAR WASH. A building or portion thereof, containing facilities for washing
motor vehicles, using automatic production line methods with a chain conveyor,
blower, steam cleaning device, or other mechanical devices, or providing space,
water, and equipment for the hand washing of autos, whether by the customer or
the operator.
CELLAR. The portion of a building which is partly or completely below grade and
having at least one-half of its height below grade.
CONFORMING BUILDING OR STRUCTURE. Any building or structure which complies with
all of the regulations of this chapter or of any amendment hereto governing
bulk for the zoning district in which such building or structure is located.
CONFORMING USE. Any use which complies with all the regulations specified in
this chapter or any subsequent amendment hereto for the zoning district in
which such use is located.
CURB LEVEL. For a building, the level of the established curb in front of such
building measured at the center of such front. Where no curb has been
established, the CURB LEVEL is the elevation of the centerline of the street
adjacent to the lot on which the building is or is to be located, measured at
the center point of the lot frontage.
DAY CARE CENTER. A premises receiving more than eight children for care during
all or part of a day or night.
DAY CARE HOME. A dwelling unit in which one or more persons provides care
during the day to not more than eight children. The maximum of eight children
includes all natural, adopted, and foster minor children of the residents of
the dwelling unit, plus any other minors who are under full-time care or
supervision in the dwelling unit.
DORMITORY. A residential building where group sleeping accommodations are
provided for persons not members of the same family and where the number
accommodated exceeds that allowed in a dwelling unit in the district. The group
sleeping accommodations may be in one room or in a series of closely associated
rooms under occupancy and single management as in college dormitories,
fraternity houses, sorority houses, military barracks, and the like, regardless
of whether meals are provided.
DISTRICT. A portion of the territory of the town within which certain generally
uniform regulations and requirements unique thereto apply under the provisions
of this chapter.
DRIVE-IN ESTABLISHMENT. A business or institution where the principal use is
either the offering of goods or services directly to customers waiting in
parked motor vehicles or carry-out food service.
DRIVEWAY. A private access way for motor vehicles between a public or private
street and one or more structures or off-street parking areas.
DWELLING. A building designed or used principally for residential occupancy
containing one or more dwelling units, including single-family, two-family, and
multiple-family dwellings.
DWELLING UNIT, ATTACHED. One which is joined to another dwelling unit on one or
more sides by a vertical party wall or walls.
DWELLING UNIT, DETACHED. One which is entirely surrounded by open space.
DWELLING, MULTIPLE-FAMILY. A building containing three or more dwelling units.
DWELLING, SINGLE-FAMILY. A building containing one dwelling unit only.
DWELLING, TWO-FAMILY. A building containing two dwelling units only.
DWELLING UNIT. One or more rooms arranged or designed for the use of one family
living together as a single housekeeping unit with cooking, living, sanitary,
and sleeping facilities in a self- contained unit.
EFFICIENCY UNIT. A dwelling unit consisting of one principal room together with
bathroom, kitchen, hallway, closets, or dining area.
FAMILY. Either one or two or more persons each related to each other by blood,
marriage, or legal adoption, and an aggregate of not more than two roomers or
boarders not related to each other, whether or not gratuitous, maintaining a
common householder in a dwelling unit; or a group of not more than four persons
not so related maintaining a common household in a dwelling unit.
FENCE. A structure other than a building or a portion thereof which is a
barrier and is used as a boundary, screen, separation, and means of privacy,
protection, or confinement and is constructed of wood, plastic, metal, wire
mesh, masonry, or comparable material.
FLOODPLAIN. Any area designated on the special flood hazard map of the Federal
Insurance Administrator designated as having special flood hazards.
FLOOD BASE ELEVATION. The elevation of the water level at the 100-year flood
level.
FLOOR AREA RATIO. The numerical value obtained through dividing the floor area
of a building or buildings by the lot area on which such building(s) are
located.
FRONTAGE. The measure of lineal contiguity along a public right-of-way as
measured along that right-of-way between a lot or portion thereof and another
lot, public street, alley, or public way.
GRADE. A reference plane representing the average finished ground level
adjoining the building at all exterior walls.
GROUND FLOOR AREA. The area of a building in square feet as measured in a
horizontal plane at the ground floor level within its largest outside
dimensions, exclusive of open porches, breezeways, terraces, garages, and
exterior stairways, furnace, and laundry areas.
HELIPORT. An area of land, water, and/or a structure or building which is used
or intended for use for the landing and taking off of helicopters, and any
appurtenant areas which are used or intended for use for helicopter buildings
or HELIPORT facilities or rights-of-way, including all necessary pads,
helicopter storage and tie-down areas, hangars, and other necessary buildings
and open spaces.
HISTORIC AREA. An area containing buildings or places in which historic events
have occurred or which have special public value because of notable
architectural or other features relating to the cultural or artistic heritage
of the community which warrant conservation and preservation.
HOME OCCUPATION. An occupation or profession practiced by a member of the
family residing in the dwelling unit, which is clearly an accessory use to the
principal residential use of the dwelling unit and which is listed in Sec.
153.127.
HOSPITAL. An institution where the sick or injured are given medical or
surgical care.
HOTEL, MOTEL, or INN. An establishment containing lodging accommodations
designed for use by transients, travelers, or temporary guests. Facilities
provided may include maid service, laundering of linen used on the premises,
telephone and secretarial or desk service, meetings rooms, and restaurants.
JUNK YARD. An open area where junk waste, scrap, used equipment and vehicle
parts, discarded or salvaged materials are bought, sold, exchanged, stored,
baled, packed, disassembled, or handled, including, but not limited to, scrap
iron and other metals, paper, rags, rubber tires, and bottles. A JUNK YARD
includes automobile wrecking yards, house wrecking yards, used lumber yards,
and places or yards for storage of salvaged house wrecking and structural steel
materials and equipment, but does not include uses established entirely within
enclosed buildings.
KENNEL. Any premises where three or more dogs, cats, or other household
domestic animals over four months of age are owned, boarded, bred, or offered
for sale.
LOT. A parcel, tract, or area of land with access from an abutting street for
the full width of the LOT, and which is occupied by or intended for occupancy
by one principal building or principal use together with any accessory
buildings and such yard area as required by this chapter. It may be a single
surveyed parcel separately described in a deed or plat which is recorded in the
office of the County Recorder, or it may include parts of, or a combination of
such surveyed parcels when adjacent to one another and used as one. In
determining lot area and boundary lines, no part under water or within the
limits of a street shall be included.
LOT AREA. The area of a horizontal plane bounded by the vertical planes through
front, side, and rear lot lines.
LOT, CORNER. A lot situated at the intersection of two streets.
LOT, DEPTH. The mean horizontal distance between the front lot line and the
rear lot line of a lot, measured within the lot boundaries.
LOT, INTERIOR. A lot other than a corner or a reversed corner lot.
LOT LINE (PROPERTY LINE). A boundary line of a lot.
LOT LINE, FRONT. The boundary line of any lot which is along a dedicated public
street or the occupation line on a non-dedicated public street. On corner lots,
the FRONT LOT LINE shall be the boundary line along such street right-of-way
line that is established at the time of application of a building permit.
LOT LINE, REAR. The lot line most nearly parallel to and most remote from the
front lot line.
LOT OF RECORD. A lot which is part of a subdivision recorded in the office of
the County Recorder of Deeds, or a lot or parcel described by metes and bounds,
the description of which has been so recorded on the date of the adoption of
this chapter.
LOT, REVERSED CORNER. A corner lot the street side lot line of which is
substantially a continuation of the front lot line of the first lot to its
rear.
LOT LINE, SIDE. Any boundary of a lot which is not a front lot line or a rear
lot line.
LOT, THROUGH. A lot having a pair of opposite lot lines along two more or less
parallel public streets, and which is not a corner lot. On a THROUGH LOT, both
street lines shall be deemed FRONT LOT LINES.
LOT WIDTH. The mean horizontal distance between the side lot lines of a lot,
measured within the lot boundary. LOT WIDTH shall be measured at the front lot
line or at the required setback line or cul-de-sac within the lot boundary
behind the required front yard setback line.
MOBILE HOME. A dwelling unit of vehicular, portable design build on a chassis
and designed to be moved from one site to another and capable of being used
with or without a permanent foundation.
MOBILE HOME PARK. A lot or lands upon which two or more independent mobile
homes are located either free of charge or for a fee, and shall include any
buildings, structure, tent, vehicle, or enclosure used or intended for use as a
part of the equipment of such MOBILE HOME PARK.
MODULAR HOME. A dwelling unit intended to be used for residential occupancy and
designed to be located on a permanent foundation as identified by the state
modular seal affixed to the electrical panel box.
NAMEPLATE. A sign indicating the name and address of a building, or the name of
an occupant thereof and/or the occupation practiced therein.
NONCONFORMING BUILDING OR STRUCTURE. Any building or structure, or portion
thereof, lawfully existing at the time of adoption of this chapter, or
amendment thereto, which does not comply with all the regulations of this
chapter or any amendment hereto governing bulk for the zoning district in which
such building or structure is located.
NONCONFORMING LOT. A lot of record which, when recorded, met the minimum lot
area and other dimension requirements of the town but which, through subsequent
amendments to such code or other acts of a public body has caused the lot of
record to be in conflict with the minimum lot area or other dimension
requirements of the codes.
NONCONFORMING USE. Any use of land, buildings, or structures which use is not
permitted in the zoning district in which such use is located, but which use
was permitted at the time such use was established.
NURSERY (GREENHOUSE). A space, building, or structure, or combination thereof,
for the storage of live trees, shrubs, or plants offered for wholesale or
retail sale on the premises, including product used for landscaping and
gardening.
NURSING HOME. A home for the aged, chronically ill, incurable persons, or a
place of rest for those suffering bodily disorders in which three or more
persons, not members of the immediate family residing on the premises, are
received, kept, or provided with food and shelter or care, but not including
hospitals, clinics, or similar institutions devoted primarily to the diagnosis
and treatment of disease or injury.
OPEN SALES LOT. Any land used or occupied for the purpose of buying and selling
merchandise, motor vehicles, any form of trailers, mobile homes, motorized
homes, motor scooters, motorcycles, boats, aircraft, and monuments for the
storing of same prior to sale.
PARKING SPACE. A space within a public or private parking area which contains
minimum dimensions of nine feet by 20 feet, exclusive of access drives or
aisles, for the purpose of vehicular storage.
PRINCIPAL BUILDING. A building in which the principal use of the lot on which
it is located and conducted, including a building that is attached to such a
building in a substantial way, such as by a roof.
PRINCIPAL USE. A primary use of land or buildings as distinguished from an
accessory use. A PRINCIPAL USE may be either permitted or special.
RIDING STABLE. The grounds and building where horses are bred, raised, trained,
boarded, or kept for remuneration, hire, or sale.
ROOMING HOUSE. Any residential building, or any part thereof, containing one or
more rooming units, in which space is let by the owner or operator to more than
five persons who do not constitute a family.
ROOMING UNIT. Any room or group of rooms forming a single habitable unit used
or intended to be used for living and sleeping, but not for cooking or eating
purposes.
ROW HOUSE (TOWNHOUSE). A multiple-family dwelling containing three or more
attached dwelling units, each on its own plot of ground and joined to one (end
unit) or two (interior units) dwelling units by firewalls. Each dwelling unit
shall occupy the internal space from the ground to the roof.
SETBACK. The horizontal distance between the front lot line and any building or
structure located on such lot.
SIGN. Any identification, description, illustration, or device, illuminated or
non-illuminated, which is visible from any public place or is located on
private property and exposed to the public and which directs attention to a
product, service, place, activity, person, institution, business, or
solicitation, including any permanently installed or situated merchandise; or
any emblem printing, banner, pennant, placard, or temporary sign designed to
advertise, identify, or convey information, with the exception of window
displays and national, state, county, municipal, and religious flags, including
goods and services, offered upon the property on which the SIGN is located.
SPECIAL USE PERMIT. A specific use of land or buildings, or both, described and
permitted herein, subject to special provisions and which, because of its
unique characteristics, cannot be properly classified as a permitted use, as
provided in Sec.
153.011.
STORY. The portion of a building included between the upper surface of a floor
and upper surface of the floor or roof next above. A basement shall be counted
as a STORY, and a cellar shall not be counted as a STORY.
STREET, PUBLIC. A dedicated and accepted public right-of-way or other public
way used and maintained by the town which, in either case, affords the
principal means of access to adjacent lots or property.
STRUCTURE. Anything constructed or erected that requires location on or in the
ground or attachment to something having a location on or in the ground.
STRUCTURAL ALTERATION. Any change, other than incidental repairs, which would
prolong the life of the supporting members of a building, such as the addition,
removal, or alteration of a bearing wall, columns, beams, girders, or
foundations.
TOURIST CAMP. A residential facility designed, used, or intended to be used to
accommodate the overnight temporary location, hook-up, or use of its facilities
for travel trailers, camp trailers.
USE. The purpose or activity for which the land, or building thereon, is
designed, arranged, or intended, or for which it is occupied or maintained, and
shall include any manner of performance of such activity with respect to the
performance standards of this chapter.
USE, PERMITTED. A use which may be lawfully established in a particular
district or districts, provided it conforms to all requirements and regulations
of such district.
VARIATION. A deviation from the bulk requirements of the zoning ordinance as
provided in Sec.
153.010.
WAREHOUSE. A structure, part thereof, or area used principally for the storage
of goods and merchandise.
WHOLESALE ESTABLISHMENT. A business establishment engaged in selling to
retailers or jobbers rather than consumers.
YARD. An open space, on the same lot with a building or structure, unoccupied
and unobstructed from its lowest level to the sky.
YARD, FRONT. The yard, required or otherwise, extending along the full length
of the front lot line between the side lot lines from the front line of the
building or structure, including any overhang features, to the front lot lines.
YARD, REAR. The yard, required or otherwise, extending along the full length of
the rear lot line between the side lot lines from the rear line of the building
or structure, including any overhang features, to the rear property line.
YARD, SIDE. The yard, required or otherwise, extending along the side lot line
between the front yard and the rear yard from the side line of the building or
structure, including any overhang features, to the side property line.
YARD, CORNER SIDE. A side yard which faces a public street.
YARD, INTERIOR SIDE. An INTERIOR SIDE YARD which is located immediately
adjacent to another lot or to any alley separating such side yard from another
lot.
ZONING ADMINISTRATOR. The person authorized by the town to administer the town
zoning ordinance.
ZONING BOARD OF APPEALS. The body authorized to enforce the town zoning
ordinance according to the powers and duties listed herein.
ZONING DISTRICT. A section or sections or incorporated territory of the town
for which the regulations and requirements governing use, lot, and bulk or
buildings and premises are uniform.
ZONING DISTRICT MAP. The portion of this chapter showing all of the zoning
district boundaries within the town.
ZONING DISTRICT MAP AMENDMENT. The procedure set forth to change the zoning
district boundaries of the zoning district map portion of this chapter.
ZONING TEXT AMENDMENT. The procedure set forth to change the text or written
portion of this chapter.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.2)
Sec. 153.005 ADMINISTRATIVE OFFICER.
   The Zoning Administrator shall be in charge of the administration and
enforcement of this chapter. The Zoning Administrator shall be appointed by the
Town Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.19.1)
Sec. 153.006 DUTIES.
   The Zoning Administrator is hereby authorized to receive, review, and issue
improvement location permits in accordance with the terms of this chapter. The
Zoning Administrator shall make the necessary site inspections to ensure
compliance with this chapter. The Zoning Administrator shall act as secretary
to the Zoning Board of Appeals.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.19.2)
Sec. 153.007 ZONING BOARD OF APPEALS.
   The Town Zoning Board of Appeals is hereby established. The Zoning Board of
Appeals is responsible for a vacancy on the Board of Zoning Appeals shall be
filled for the unexpired term of the member whose place has become vacant, such
vacancy to be filled under the same procedure as in an original appointment.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.19.3)
Sec. 153.008 MEETINGS.
   All meetings of the Zoning Board of Appeals shall be called by the
Chairperson and held at the Town Hall or other locations as announced. All
meetings of the Board of Zoning Appeals shall be open to the public. The Board
of Zoning Appeals shall keep minutes of its proceedings, showing the vote of
each member upon every question, or if absent for each case reviewed by the
Zoning Board of Appeals. At all public hearings before the Board of Zoning
Appeals, said Board shall require that all testimony by witnesses be given
under oath.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.19.4)
Sec. 153.009 APPEAL AND REVIEW.
   The Zoning Board of Appeals shall hear and decide appeals from and review
any order, requirement, and decision of determination made by the Zoning
Administrator, who is charged with the enforcement of this chapter. When an
appeal is taken from the Zoning Administrator by an aggrieved person or party,
the Chairperson of the Zoning Board of Appeals shall place the appeal on the
meeting docket and shall prepare a notice of appeal specifying the grounds
thereof and proceed under the public hearing section of this chapter. An appeal
stays all proceedings in furtherance of the action appealed from. The
concurring votes of five members of the Zoning Board of Appeals shall be
required to reverse any order, requirement, decision, or determination of the
Zoning Administrator, enforcing the town zoning ordinance and to hold any
public hearings as required under the provisions herein. The members of the
Zoning Board of Appeals and the Chairperson shall be appointed by the Town
Board of Trustees. The Zoning Board of Appeals shall consist of seven members
to serve respectively for the following terms: one for one year, one for two
years, one for three years, one for four years, and three for five years, the
successor to each member to initially appoint to serve for a term of five
years. All members of the Zoning Board of Appeals shall be residents of the
town.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.19.5)
Sec. 153.010 VARIATIONS.
   (1)   Authority. The Zoning Board of Appeals may permit variations in
specific cases where practical difficulties or particular hardships occur. The
Board of Zoning Appeals shall do so only where the granting of such a variation
would be in harmony with the general purpose and intent of this chapter. The
Board of Zoning Appeals may grant variations in the following instances only,
and in no others:
      (A)   To permit any yard, court, setback, or line to be of less dimension
than required by the applicable regulations;
      (B)   To permit any structure to exceed the height limitations imposed by
the applicable regulations;
      (C)   To permit a reduction in the number of off-street parking spaces in
connection with a particular use; and/or
      (D)   To permit a reduction in the minimum ground floor area requirement
for single-family residences.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.20.1)
   (2)   Public hearings. No variation shall be granted by the Zoning Board of
Appeals except after a public hearing is conducted as provided in Sec.
153.012.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.20.2)
   (3)   Standards. The Zoning Board of Appeals shall prepare a record of the
findings of fact and decision in each case from the evidence presented at the
public hearing. The Board of Zoning Appeals shall use the following standards
when considering the practical difficulties or particular hardships.
      (A)   The special conditions and circumstances exist which are peculiar
to the land, structure, or building involved and which are not applicable to
other lands, structures, or buildings in the same district.
      (B)   The land, structure, or building involved cannot reasonably be
developed or used in accordance with the requirements of the district in which
it is located.
      (C)   The literal interpretation of the provisions of this chapter would
deprive the applicant of rights commonly enjoyed by other properties in the
same district under the terms of this chapter.
      (D)   The special condition and circumstances do not result from the
actions of the applicant.
      (E)   Granting the variation requested will not give the applicant of the
property in question privileges not generally held by other property owners or
occupants in the same vicinity and district.
      (F)   The granting of the variation will be in harmony with the purpose
and intent of this chapter, and will not be injurious to the neighborhood, or
otherwise detrimental to the public welfare.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.20.3)
Sec. 153.011 SPECIAL USE PERMITS.
   (1)   Authority. Certain types of uses may be permitted in the particular
zoning districts when special consideration has been given to the site itself
and the characteristics of surrounding property. The purpose of this section is
to allow the Zoning Board of Appeals the authority to impose certain
conditional standards when taking into consideration the characteristics of the
surrounding property.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.21.1)
   (2)   Public hearing. No special use permits shall be granted by the Zoning
Board of Appeals except after a public hearing is conducted as provided in Sec.
153.012.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.21.2)
   (3)   Standards. The Zoning Board of Appeals shall prepare a record of the
findings of act and decision in each case from the evidence presented at the
public hearing. The Board of Zoning Appeals shall use the following standards
when considering an application for a special use permit:
      (A)   The proposed use is consistent with the Town Land Use Plan;
      (B)   The proposed use will not be detrimental to or endanger the public
health, safety, morals, comfort, or general welfare;
      (C)   The proposed use will not be injurious to the use and enjoyment of
other property in the immediate vicinity for the uses already permitted, or
substantially reduce the value of neighboring property;
      (D)   The proposed use will not impede orderly growth, development, and
improvement of surrounding properties for those uses permitted in the zoning
district; and
      (E)   Is provided or will be provided with adequate utilities, access
roads, and drainage and necessary facilities.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.21.3)
Sec. 153.012 PUBLIC HEARING PROCEDURES.
   All applications for a variation, special use permit, and zoning text or
zoning district map amendment shall be filed with the Town Clerk. The Town
Clerk shall forward the applications to the Zoning Board of Appeals for
processing. No variation, special use permit, or zoning text or zoning district
map amendment shall be processed except after a public hearing before the
Zoning Board of Appeals. Public notice stating the time and place of the public
hearing shall be published at least once, not more than 30 or less than 15 days
before the public hearing in one or more newspapers published in the town, or
in one or more newspapers of general circulation if a newspaper is not
published in the town. The costs or charges of the publication notice shall be
paid by the applicant.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.23)
Sec. 153.013 IMPROVEMENT LOCATION PERMITS.
   (1)   Within the town, no structure or improvement, home occupation, or use
of land may be altered, changed, placed, erected, or located on platted or
unplanted land, unless the structure, improvement, or use, and its location,
conform with this chapter, and an improvement location permit for such
structure, improvement or use has been issued by the Zoning Administrator. The
Zoning Administrator shall issue an improvement location permit upon written
application, when the proposed structure, improvement, or use and its location
conform in all respects with the requirements of this chapter.
   (2)   Any person, or corporation which makes application for an improvement
location permit, shall at the time of making such application, furnish the
Zoning Administrator with a plot plan, drawn to scale as required by the Zoning
Administrator, and show the following items:
      (A)   The location and size of all buildings or structures already on the
site and those to be erected;
      (B)   The width of all entrances to and exits from the site;
      (C)   All adjacent streets and roadways; and
      (D)   Any additional information requested by the Zoning Administrator.
When an application for an improvement location permit is filed with the Zoning
Administrator, it shall be accompanied with a fee set in accordance with the
schedule of fees adopted by the Town Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.24) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.014 FEES.
   The Town Board of Trustees shall establish by separate ordinance a schedule
of fees indicating the charges for an improvement location permit, variations,
special use permits, zoning text or zoning map amendments, and appeals. The
schedule of fees may be amended by the Town Board of Trustees.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.25)
ZONING DISTRICTS
Sec. 153.025 DISTRICTS DESIGNATED; BOUNDARIES.
   (1)   Designation.
      (A)   The town is hereby classified and divided into zoning districts
designated as follows:
District Designation Primarily For
District Designation Primarily For
AG                   Agriculture District
C-1                  Local Business District
C-2                  Central Business District
I-1                  Light Industry District
I-2                  General Industry District
MH                   Mobile Home District
R-1                  Single-Family Residence District
R-2                  Medium Density Residential District
 
      (B)   The zoning district map, which accompanies and is hereby declared
to be a part of this chapter, shows the boundaries of and the area covered by
the zoning districts.
   (2)   Boundaries.
      (A)   The boundaries of the districts established by this section are as
shown on the zoning map, which is a part of this chapter.
      (B)   When the exact boundaries of a district are uncertain, they shall
be determined by use of the scale of the zoning map.
      (C)   When a right-of-way is vacated, the districts adjoining each side
are respectively extended to the center of the area so vacated.
      (D)   In the case of further uncertainty, the Board of Zoning Appeals
shall interpret the intent of the zoning map as to the location of the boundary
in question.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.3)
Sec. 153.026 AG AGRICULTURAL.
   The AG District is established to include lands for agricultural.
   (1)   Uses permitted. The following uses shall be permitted in the AG
District, plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be
similar in nature. All uses shall be subject to the property development
standards in division (3) below and Sec.
153.125:
      (A)   Advertising structure;
      (B)   Commercial greenhouse;
      (C)   Dwelling, single-family;
      (D)   Farm, general;
      (E)   Farm equipment, sales, and service;
      (F)   Home occupation;
      (G)   Municipal or government building;
      (H)   Public park or recreational facilities;
      (I)   Railroad right-of-way and necessary uses;
      (J)   Roadside produce sales stand;
      (K)   School, public or private;
      (L)   Signs; and
      (M)   Warehouse and storage of grain, seed, and dry fertilizer, including
grain storage and drying facilities.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.4.1)
   (2)   Special uses. The following uses may be permitted by special use
permit as provided for in Sec.
153.011:
      (A)   Airport;
      (B)   Anhydrous ammonia or similar liquefied fertilizer, and bulk
fertilizer storage and distribution (commercial);
      (C)   Asphalt or asphaltic concrete batching plant;
      (D)   Assembly halls for use by nonprofit organizations;
      (E)   Auction sales yard;
      (F)   Bottled gas, storage and distribution;
      (G)   Bulk storage of petroleum products;
      (H)   Camping facilities;
      (I)   Cemetery;
      (J)   Church;
      (K)   Contractor’s storage yard;
      (L)   Country club or golf course;
      (M)   Farm, confinement feeding;
      (N)   Kennel;
      (O)   Lodge or private club;
      (P)   Machine shop;
      (Q)   Mineral extraction;
      (R)   Private recreational development;
      (S)   Public or commercial sanitary landfill;
      (T)   Race tract (outdoor);
      (U)   Riding stable;
      (V)   Seasonal hunting or fishing lodge;
      (W)   Shooting range (outdoor);
      (X)   Slaughterhouse;
      (Y)   Theater (outdoor); and
      (Z)   Welding shop.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.4.2)
   (3)   Property development standards. The following property development
standards shall apply to all land and structures in the AG Agriculture
District.
      (A)   Minimum lot area. All lots hereafter created in this district shall
contain an area of not less than 30,000 square feet.
      (B)   Lot dimensions. All lots hereafter created shall have a minimum
width of 150 feet and a minimum depth of 200 feet. Curve and cul-de-sac lot
width shall be measured as chord distance at the building setback line.
      (C)   Building height. No building or structure, located in this
district, may be changed or erected so as to have a height greater than 35
feet.
      (D)   Yards. No building shall be erected or enlarged unless the
following yard setback requirements are provided:
   (1)   A front yard of not less than 40 feet;
   (2)   The minimum width of each side yard for the principal building shall
not be less than 25 feet; and
   (3)   The minimum depth of a rear yard for the principal building shall not
be less than 50 feet.
      (E)   Lot coverage. The buildings on a lot, including accessory
buildings, may not exceed in coverage 30% of the total lot area.
      (F)   Off-street parking. The provisions of Sec.
153.060 to
153.062 shall apply.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.4.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.027 R-1 SINGLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL.
   The R-1 District is established to provide areas for single-family
residential development.
   (1)   Uses permitted. The following uses shall be permitted in the R-
1 District plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be
similar in nature. All uses shall be subject to the property development
standards in division (3) below and Sec.
153.125:
      (A)   Dwelling, single-family;
      (B)   Home occupation;
      (C)   Municipal or government building; and
      (D)   Public park or recreational facilities.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.5.1)
   (2)   Special uses. The following uses may be permitted by special use
permit as provided for in Sec.
153.011:
      (A)   Church;
      (B)   Country club or golf course; and
      (C)   Kindergarten or day nursery (other than as a part of a school,
providing other grades).
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.5.2)
   (3)   Property development standards. The following property development
standards shall apply to all land and structures in the R-1 Single-Family
Residential District.
      (A)   Minimum lot area. All lots hereafter created in this district shall
contain an area of not less than 7,200 square feet.
      (B)   Lot dimensions. All lots hereafter created shall have a minimum
width of 60 feet and a minimum depth of 120 feet. Curve and cul-de-sac lot
width shall be measured as chord distance at the building setback line.
      (C)   Building height. No building or structure, located in this
district, may be changed or erected so as to have a height greater than 30
feet.
      (D)   Minimum ground floor area. No single-family dwelling may be changed
or erected so that its ground floor area is less than 750 square feet.
      (E)   Yards. No building shall be erected or enlarged unless the
following yard setback requirements are provided:
   (1)   A front yard of not less than 20 feet;
   (2)   The minimum width of each side yard for the principal building shall
not be less than ten feet; and
   (3)   The minimum depth of a rear yard for the principal building shall not
be less than 20 feet.
      (F)   Lot coverage. The buildings on lot, including accessory buildings,
may not exceed in coverage 35% of the total lot area.
      (G)   Off-street parking. The provisions of Sec.
153.060 to
153.062 shall apply.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.5.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.028 R-2 MEDIUM DENSITY RESIDENTIAL.
   (1)   Uses permitted. The following uses shall be permitted in the R-
2 District, plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be
similar in nature. All uses shall be subject to the property development
standards in division (3) below and Sec.
153.125:

(Prior Code, Sec. 21.6.1)
   (2)   Special uses. The following uses may be permitted by special use
permit as provided in Sec.
153.011:
      (A)   Boarding or lodging house;
      (B)   Church;
      (C)   Home for the aged; and
      (D)   Nursing home, orphanage.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.6.2)
   (3)   Property development standards. The following property development
standards shall apply to all land and structures in the R-2 Medium Density
Residential District.
      (A)   Minimum lot area. All lots hereafter created in this district shall
contain an area of not less than 8,000 square feet.
      (B)   Lot dimensions. All lots hereafter created shall have a minimum
width of 60 feet and a minimum depth of 100 feet. Curve and cul-de-sac lot
width shall be measured as chord distance at the building setback line.
      (C)   Building height. No building or structure, located in this
district, may be changed or erected so as to have a height greater than 40
feet.
      (D)   Minimum ground floor area. No single-family dwelling may be changed
or erected so that its ground floor area is less than 750 square feet.
      (E)   Yards. No building shall be erected or enlarged unless the
following yard setback requirements are provided:
         (1)   A front yard of not less than 20 feet;
         (2)   The minimum width of each side yard for the principal building
shall not be less than ten feet; and
         (3)   The minimum depth of a rear yard for the principal building
shall not be less than 20 feet.
      (F)   Lot coverage. The building on a lot, including accessory buildings,
may not exceed in coverage 35% of the total lot area.
      (G)   Off-street parking. The provisions of Sec.
153.060 to
153.062 shall apply.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.6.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.029 MH MOBILE HOME.
   (1)   Uses permitted. The following uses shall be permitted in the MH
District, plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be
similar in nature. All uses shall be subject to the property development
standards in division (2) below and Sec.
153.125:
      (A)   Accessory uses and buildings;
      (B)   Home occupation;
      (C)   Mobile home; and
      (D)   Mobile home park.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.7.1)
   (2)   Property development standards. The following property development
standards shall apply to all land and structures in the MH District. However,
the standards of the State Mobile Home Park Act (Ill. Rev. Stat. 711—736, as
amended from time to time, hereafter) shall supersede these standards if the
standards included in the Act are more stringent.
      (A)   Minimum lot area. No minimum requirement.
      (B)   Yards. No building shall be erected or enlarged unless the
following yard setback requirements are provided:
   (1)   A front yard of not less than ten feet;
   (2)   The minimum width of each side yard for the principal building shall
not be less than ten feet; and
   (3)   The minimum depth of a rear yard for the principal building shall not
be less than ten feet.
      (C)   Off-street parking. There shall be two off-street parking spaces
provided for each mobile home stand.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.7.2) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999

153.011:

shall not be less than ten feet; and
         (3)   The minimum depth of a rear yard for the principal building
shall not be less than ten feet.
      (E)   Off-street parking. The provision of this chapter shall apply.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.8.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.031 C-2 CENTRAL BUSINESS.
   The C-2 District is established to provide areas of retail trade that are
associated with the Town Central Business District.
   (1)   Uses permitted. The following uses shall be permitted in the C-
2 District, plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be
similar in nature. All uses shall be subject to the property development
standards in division (3) below and Sec.
153.125:
      (A)   All uses permitted in the C-1 District;
      (B)   Assembly halls for nonprofit organizations;
      (C)   Automobile sales;
      (D)   Billiard room;
      (E)   Boarding or lodging house;
      (F)   Charitable institutions;
      (G)   Department store;
      (H)   Dressmaking shop;
      (I)   Flower shop;
      (J)   Hotel or motel (excluding apartments);
      (K)   Municipal or government building;
      (L)   Newsdealer;
      (M)   Night club;
      (N)   Nursing home;
      (O)   Office supplies;
      (P)   Photograph studio;
      (Q)   Print shop and newspaper;
      (R)   Railway or motor bus station;
      (S)   Studio business (art, interior decorating, music, and the like);
      (T)   Tavern; and
      (U)   Theater, indoor.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.9.1)
   (2)   Special uses. The following uses may be permitted by special use
permit as provided for in Sec.
153.011:
      (A)   Apartment (located on second story);
      (B)   Farm equipment sales and service; and
      (C)   Locker, cold storage for individual use.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.9.2)
   (3)   Property development standards. The following property development
standards shall apply to all land and structures in the C-2 Central Business
District.
      (A)   Minimum lot area. No minimum requirement.
      (B)   Lot dimensions. No minimum requirement.
      (C)   Building height. No building or structure, located in this
district, may be changed or erected so as to have a height greater than 40
feet.
      (D)   Yards. No building shall be erected or enlarged unless the
following yard setback requirements are provided.
   (1)   Front yard. No minimum requirement.
   (2)   Side yards. No minimum requirement.
   (3)   Rear yard. No minimum requirement.
      (E)   Off-street parking. The provision of this chapter shall apply.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.9.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.032 I-1 LIGHT INDUSTRY.
   The I-1 District is established to provide areas for light industrial and
restricted retail trades.
   (1)   Uses permitted. The following uses shall be permitted in the I-
1 District, plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be
similar in nature. All uses shall be subject to the property development
standards in division (3) below and Sec.
153.125:
      (A)   Advertising structures;
      (B)   Automobile repair;
      (C)   Automobile service center;
      (D)   Bank;
      (E)   Boat sales, service, storage, and rental;
      (F)   Contractor’s storage yard;
      (G)   Industrial park;
      (H)   Industry, light;
      (I)   Kennel;
      (J)   Lumber yard;
      (K)   Mobile home or trailer sales area;
      (L)   Municipal or government building;
      (M)   Police or fire station;
      (N)   Restaurant;
      (O)   Truck freight;
      (P)   Terminal truck service center; and
      (Q)   Warehouse and storage of grain, seed, and dry fertilizer storage
facilities, including drain storage and drying.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.10.1)
   (2)   Special uses. The following uses may be permitted by special use
permit as provided in Sec.
153.011:

      (C)   Automobile laundry;
      (D)   Bottled gas, storage and distribution;
      (E)   Bulk storage of petroleum products;
      (F)   Mineral extraction; and
      (G)   Slaughterhouse.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.10.2)
   (3)   Property development standards. The following property development
standards shall apply to all land and structures in the I-1 Light Industrial
District.
      (A)   Minimum lot area. No minimum requirement.
      (B)   Lot dimensions. All lots hereafter created shall have a minimum
width of 50 feet. Curve and cul-de-sac lot width shall be measured as chord
distance at the building setback line.
      (C)   Building height. No building or structure, located in this
district, may be changed or erected so as to have a height greater than 40
feet.
      (D)   Yards. No building shall be erected or enlarged unless the
following yard setback requirements are provided.
         (1)   Front yard. No minimum requirement.
         (2)   Side yards. No minimum requirement.
         (3)   Rear yard. No minimum requirement.
      (E)   Off-street parking. The provision of this chapter shall apply.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.10.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999

2 District, plus such other uses as the Board of Zoning Appeals may deem to be

153.125:

      (C)   Contractor’s storage yard;

   (2)   Special uses. The following uses may be permitted by special use
permit as provided for in Sec.
153.011:

distribution (commercial);
      (B)   Asphalt and asphalitic concrete batch plant;
      (C)   Bottled gas, storage and distribution;
      (D)   Bulk storage of petroleum products;
      (E)   Junk yard;
      (F)   Manufacturing, storage, or use of explosives;
      (G)   Mineral extraction;
      (H)   Petroleum tank farm, commercial;

standards shall apply to all land and structures in the I-2 General Industry
District: Minimum lot area: no minimum requirement.

   Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
SIGNS AND OUTDOOR ADVERTISING STRUCTURES
Sec. 153.045 GENERAL PROVISIONS.
   Signs and outdoor advertising structures may be erected in the zoning
districts where such use is permitted subject to the following provisions.
   (1)   No sign or outdoor advertising structure shall be located so as to
endanger the health and safety of operators or motor vehicles on the public
streets.
   (2)   Signs or advertising structures may be lighted in a reasonable manner
that make the structure visible, but does not create substantial glare. No sign
may be erected that flashes, uses sound or other mechanical devices, or that
uses unusual lighting or other means of illumination.
   (3)   No sign or advertising structure shall project into the public right-
of-way or other adjacent property.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.14.1) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.046 PERMITTED SIGNS; RESIDENTIAL DISTRICTS.
   In residential zoning districts, non-illuminated nameplates for home
occupations are permitted, provided the nameplate does not exceed one square
foot in area, and is used for indicating the name and address of the occupant
or home occupation.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.14.2) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.047 PERMITTED SIGNS; COMMERCIAL AND INDUSTRIAL DISTRICTS.
   In commercial and industrial zoning districts, signs are permitted for the
purpose of identifying or describing the nature or type of use located on the
premises, with the sign subject to the following.
   (1)   Signs may be free-standing, attached to the building, or painted on
the wall of a building.
   (2)   No sign shall be erected or located to have a total height greater
than 20 feet above the level of the street upon which the sign faces, nor shall
the signable area of any such sign exceed 100 feet.
   (3)   Free-standing signs may contain double frontage signable areas.
However, only one sign may be located or painted on a building or premises.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.14.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.048 OUTDOOR ADVERTISING STRUCTURES.
   Outdoor advertising structures are permitted in certain zoning districts for
the purpose of advertising businesses and uses not located on the premises with
the structure, subject to the following.
   (1)   Outdoor advertising structures shall be free-standing and not attached
to or painted on any part of a building or premises.
   (2)   No outdoor advertising structure shall be erected to have a total
height greater than 20 feet above the level of the structure upon which the
structure exceeds 150 square feet.
   (3)   Double frontage signable areas are permitted.
   (4)   Only one outdoor advertising structure shall be erected on any
individual lot or parcel.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.14.4) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
OFF-STREET PARKING
Sec. 153.060 PURPOSE.
   The following regulations are established to lessen congestion in the public
streets and to set standards for the off-street parking of vehicles, taking
into consideration the particular type of use.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.15.1)
Sec. 153.061 EXISTING USES.
   Whenever the existing use of a building or structure erected prior to the
effective date of this chapter is hereafter changed substantially to a new use,
the off-street parking requirements shall be met, or in the event the floor
area, seating capacity, number of dwelling units, or other factors affecting
the parking requirements is changed.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.15.2) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.062 SCHEDULE OF PARKING REQUIREMENTS.
   For the following uses, accessory off-street parking spaces shall be
provided as required below.
   (1)   Residential uses.
      (A)   One-family dwellings and two-family dwellings. Two parking spaces
shall be provided for each dwelling unit.
      (B)   Multiple-family dwellings. Two parking spaces shall be provided for
each single dwelling unit.
      (C)   Motels, inns, and auto courts. One parking space shall be provided
for each guest or sleeping room of suite.
      (D)   Hotels. One parking space for each dwelling unit or lodging room.
      (E)   Lodging, rooming, and boarding houses. One parking space shall be
provided for each two lodging rooms.
   (2)   Retail and service uses.
      (A)   Retail stores and banks. One parking space shall be provided for
each 200 square feet of floor area.
      (B)   Automobile service stations. One parking space shall be provided
for each two employees.
      (C)   Establishments dispensing food or beverage for consumption on the
premises. One parking space shall be provided for each 300 square feet of floor
area.
      (D)   Furniture and appliance stores, household equipment or furniture
repair shops. One parking space shall be provided for each 600 square feet of
floor area.
      (E)   Motor vehicle sales and machinery sales. One parking space shall be
provided for each 300 square feet of floor area.
      (F)   Theaters (indoor). One parking space shall be provided for each
five seats.
   (3)   Offices; business, professional, and governmental. One parking space
shall be provided for each 200 square feet of floor area.
   (4)   Medical and dental clinics. Three parking spaces shall be provided for
each doctor.
   (5)   Wholesale establishments (not including warehouses and storage
buildings other than accessory). One parking space shall be provided for each
600 square feet of floor space.
   (6)   Manufacturing uses or any establishments engaged in production,
processing, cleaning, servicing, testing, or repair of materials, goods, or
products. One parking space shall be provided for each two employees.
   (7)   Warehouses and storage buildings. One parking space shall be provided
for each two employees.
   (8)   Community service uses.
      (A)   Church, school, college, and other institutional auditoriums. One
parking space shall be provided for each three auditorium seats. Adequate space
shall also be provided for buses used in connection with the activities of the
institution, and all loading and unloading of passengers shall take place upon
the premises.
      (B)   Colleges, universities, and business, professional, and trade
schools. One parking space shall be provided for each three employees, and one
parking space shall be provided for each four students, based on the minimum
number of students attending classes on the premises at any one time during any
24-hour period.
      (C)   Hospitals. One parking space shall be provided for each two
hospital beds, plus one parking space for each two employees (other than staff
doctors), plus one parking space for each doctor assigned to the staff.
      (D)   Libraries, art galleries, and museums (public). One parking space
shall be provided for each 1,000 square feet of gross floor area.
      (E)   Municipal or privately owned recreation buildings or community
centers. One parking space shall be provided for each two employees.
      (F)   Public utility and public service uses. One parking space shall be
provided for each three employees.
      (G)   Schools (nursery, elementary, and high). One parking space shall be
provided for each employee.
   (9)   Places of assembly; stadiums, arenas, auditoriums (other than church,
college, or institutional schools), convention halls, dance halls, exhibition
halls, skating rinks, and other similar places of assembly. Parking spaces
equal in number of 25% of the capacity in persons shall be provided.
   (10)   Miscellaneous uses.
      (A)   Fraternities, sororities, and dormitories. One parking space shall
be provided for each five active members.
      (B)   Private clubs and lodges (without sleeping facilities for guests).
Parking spaces equal in number to 10% of the capacity in persons shall be
provided.
      (C)   Rest homes and nursing homes. One parking space shall be provided
for each four beds, plus one parking space for each two employees (other than
staff doctors), plus one parking space for each doctor assigned to staff.
      (D)   Sanitariums, convalescent homes, or institutions for the aged or
for children. One parking space shall be provided for each four beds, plus one
parking space for each two employees (other than staff doctors), plus one
parking space for each doctor assigned to the staff.
      (E)   Theaters (automobile drive-in). Reservoir parking equal to 10% of
the vehicle capacity of such theaters shall be provided.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.15.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
ACCESSORY BUILDINGS AND USES
Sec. 153.075 LOCATION.
   (1)   Accessory uses and buildings may be permitted in all zoning districts.
   (2)   Accessory buildings shall be permitted only in side and rear yards,
and shall be located no closer than five feet to the side or rear lot lines.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.16.1) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.076 HEIGHT OF ACCESSORY BUILDINGS.
   No accessory building shall exceed a total height of 14 feet.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.16.2) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.077 CONSTRUCTION OF ACCESSORY BUILDINGS.
   An accessory building shall not be constructed before the principal building
is constructed.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.16.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.078 AREA OF ACCESSORY BUILDINGS.
   No accessory building shall exceed 1,200 square feet in area.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.16.4) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.079 RESIDENTIAL STORAGE BUILDINGS.
   (1)   Number of accessory buildings/storage buildings/detached garages shall
be a maximum of two per residence.
   (2)   Storage buildings cannot be used as dwelling units (no one may live in
the storage building).
   (3)   The building must be built of typical building materials. Unpainted
wood, plastic, plain concrete blocks, corrugated metal, plastics, canvas,
vinyl, or similar materials are not allowed.
   (4)   The roof of an accessory building cannot be corrugated metal, plastic,
or canvas. Roofs must be either composition shingles, rolled roofing, built-up
roof, metal panels type, or other material allowed by the Zoning Official.
   (5)   Accessory buildings on corner lots must meet the same setback
requirements as the main structure (house). In most cases, a minimum 20-foot
setback is required from the side property line.
   (6)   Accessory buildings that are located behind the house must be a
minimum three feet from side and rear yard property lines.
   (7)   Accessory buildings that are between the houses and the side property
line must meet the setback requirements as set forth in either the
comprehensive zoning or the planned development standards set forth for your
subdivision; however, in most cases, it will be five feet from the side
property line.
   (8)   Buildings larger than 144 square feet must be a minimum of six feet
from the house.
   (9)   Buildings cannot have a wall height taller than ten and one-half feet
and cannot have an average height taller than 15 feet.
   (10)   When the building is not attached to a normal foundation by its
plates, tie-downs shall be required in each corner.
   (11)   Accessory buildings that exceed 192 square feet in area require a
permit.
   (12)   No POD, truck boxes, or enclosed trailers shall be used as storage
buildings.
   (13)   Any violations of this section shall be governed by the nuisance
ordinance.
(Ord. -, passed 2-9-2017) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
AMENDMENTS AND CHANGES
Sec. 153.090 AUTHORITY.
   (1)   The regulations set forth in the text of the zoning ordinance and the
zoning district map may be amended upon application by any person or party, or
by the Town Board of Trustees.
   (2)   The ordinance shall only be amended when the proposed change promotes
the public health, safety, morals, comfort, or general welfare of the residents
of the town.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.22.1)
Sec. 153.091 PUBLIC HEARING.
   No zoning text amendment or zoning district map amendment shall be
considered except after a public hearing has been conducted by the Zoning Board
of Appeals as provided in Sec.
153.012.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.22.2)
Sec. 153.092 STANDARDS.
   (1)   The Zoning Board of Appeals shall prepare a record of the findings of
fact and decision in each case from the evidence presented at the public
hearing.
   (2)   The Board of Zoning Appeals shall use the following standards when
considering amendments to the ordinance:
      (A)   The suitability of the subject property for uses authorized by the
existing zoning;
      (B)   The suitability of the subject property for uses authorized by the
proposed zoning;
      (C)   The existing land uses of nearby property;
      (D)   Existing zoning of nearby property; and
      (E)   Whether the petitioned zoning change is in the best interest of the
general public or is it solely for the benefit of the petitioner.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.22.3)
Sec. 153.093 DECISION.
   After the public hearing, the Zoning Board of Appeals shall make a
recommendation to the Town Board of Trustees to approve or deny the proposed
amendment. The Town Board of Trustees shall have the final authority to approve
any amendments to the zoning district map or the zoning ordinance text. All
decisions of the Town Board of Trustees on amendments shall be final and are
subject to review in the courts of the state.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.22.4)
FENCES
Sec. 153.105 GENERAL.
   No fence may be erected in a residential, commercial, or industrial district
which does not comply with the following.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.106 PERMIT.
A permit will be required for all fences erected in the town.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.107 FRONT YARD.
   Any fence erected in a required front yard shall be at least 60% open. No
fence erected in a required front yard may exceed four feet in height, measured
from ground level. No privacy fence may be erected in the front yard of any
residence.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.108 PRIVACY FENCE.
   Any privacy fence erected on the street side of a corner lot shall be
restricted to the required rear yard, subject to the provisions of Sec.
153.109. However, a chain link or ornamental fence which is no more than four
feet in height, measured from the ground level, and which is 60% open may
extend to the front property line. No fence may be built across or on a utility
easement.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.109 SETBACKS.
   Any fence placed on an interior side yard or rear yard may be placed one
foot from the property line. Fences shall also follow town ordinances.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.110 HEIGHT.
   No fence may be erected which exceeds six feet in height above ground level
in a residential area. In commercial and industrial areas, no fence may be
erected which exceeds eight feet in height. No fence shall be erected which
exceeds the total height of five feet within 50 feet of a street line on a
corner lot.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.111 ANCHORING.
   The fence posts or other anchoring devices must be a minimum of three feet
deep when constructing a fence.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.112 MATERIALS.
   No fence may be constructed with barbed wire, metal spikes, or any other
sharp pointed materials. All chain link fences shall be installed with the
knuckle portion of the fence up and with the barb portion of the fence at or
near the ground. No fence may be electrified. Fence may be constructed of wood,
chain link, PVC, rod iron, cast metal, bushes, or other plants, stone, or other
building material that the Zoning Administrator deems acceptable. All hardware
that is used to construct or hold fence together shall face the property owner,
and the finished side shall face outward from the property owner.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.113 MAINTENANCE.
   (1)   The finished side of all fences shall face outward from the property
owner. If the fence faces an alley, it is optional.
   (2)   All areas of a lot on the exterior side of the fence, including the
fence itself, shall be maintained by the property owner.
(Ord. 394, passed 8-10-2010) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
ADDITIONAL REGULATIONS
Sec. 153.125 PROPERTY DEVELOPMENT STANDARDS.
   (1)   All lots hereafter created shall contain frontage along a public
street, and as further specified in the individual zoning districts.
   (2)   On corner lots, reversed corner lots, and double frontage lots, two
front yard setback distances shall be required as specified in the individual
zoning districts.
   (3)   No structure or use of land subject to the requirements of this
chapter shall violate any other law of the town, county, or state.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.17) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.126 NONCONFORMING BUILDING AND USE SPECIFICATIONS.
   (1)   Upon the effective date of this chapter, the lawful use of a building
or premises may be continued although such use does not conform to all the
provisions thereof, except as hereinafter provided. A nonconforming use may be
changed to another nonconforming use of the same or greater restrictions,
provided no structural changes are made in the building. Whenever a
nonconforming use has been changed to a conforming use, it shall not thereafter
be changed except in accordance with the requirements of the district.
   (2)   Any building, or building and land combination, in or on which a
nonconforming use of a building is existing shall not hereafter be expanded or
altered in any manner except in accordance with the requirements of the
district in which it is located. In the event that a nonconforming use of any
building or premises is discontinued for a period of one year, the use of the
same shall hereafter conform to the uses permitted in the district in which it
is located. When a building containing a nonconforming use is damaged by fire,
explosion, or other acts of God, to the extent of more than 50% of its assessed
value, it shall not be restored except in conformity with the regulations of
the district within which it is located. These provisions apply in the same
manner to a use which may become a nonconforming use due to a later amendment
to these regulations.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.18) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.127 HOME OCCUPATIONS.
   (1)   General. In all districts permitting residential dwellings, home
occupations shall be permitted, as long as the home occupation is incidental
and secondary to the permitted residential use of the lot.
   (2)   Permitted home occupations.
      (A)   Domestic crafts, such as a seamstress, sewing, tailoring, weaving,
washing, and ironing, carpentry work, barber shops (one chair operation only),
beauty shops (one chair operation only), repair of small household appliances,
and offices used predominantly for personal, as opposed to business use;
      (B)   Music and dance instruction, limited to one pupil at any one time;
      (C)   Private tutoring, limited to three pupils at any one time; and
      (D)   Use of premises by a medical or osteopathic physician, surgeon,
dentist, podiatrist, or chirpodist, for consultation or emergency treatment,
but not for general practice of such profession.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.12.1)
   (3)   Occupations not included. A home occupation shall not be interpreted
to include automobile repair and tune-up, offices, clinics, welding shops,
tourist homes, animal hospitals, kennels, and television repair shops.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.12.2)
   (4)   Home occupation requirements.
      (A)   Such one occupation shall be conducted entirely within the dwelling
unit used as the residence.
      (B)   The operator conducting the home occupation shall be the sole
entrepreneur.
      (C)   The primary use of the dwelling unit shall remain for residential
purposes and the operator of the home occupation shall remain a resident in the
dwelling unit.
      (D)   The home occupation shall be incidental and secondary to the
permitted residential use of the lot.
      (E)   No structural additions, enlargements, or exterior alterations
changing the residential appearance to a business or commercial appearance
shall be permitted.
      (F)   No additional and separate entrance incongruent with the
residential structural design shall be constructed for the purpose of
conducting the home occupation.
      (G)   The use shall not generate pedestrian or vehicular traffic beyond
that normal to the district in which it is located.
      (H)   Not more than one room in the dwelling unit shall be employed for
the home occupation, at grade or above grade, or 300 square feet of basement
floor area below grade.
      (I)   No provision for off-street parking or loading facilities, other
than the requirements of the residential district in which the use is located,
shall be permitted, and no part of a minimum required yard shall be used for
such off-street parking or loading purposes. No additional driveways to serve
such home occupations shall be permitted.
      (J)   No display of goods or external evidence of the home occupation
shall be permitted other than one attached, non-illuminated sign, not exceeding
one square foot in area.
      (K)   No stock, in trade or commodities, other than those prepared,
produced, or created on the premises by the operator of the home occupation,
shall be kept or sold on the premises.
      (L)   No equipment or process shall be used in such home occupation which
creates noise, vibration, glare, fumes, odors, or electrical interference
(visually or audibly), outside of the dwelling.
(Prior Code, 21.12.3) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.128 MOBILE HOMES.
   Mobile homes, trailers, or vans may be utilized in all districts as
contractor’s officers, watchperson’s shelters, or tool or equipment storage
only on the site and only during the period of construction of improvement
projects.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.13) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.129 SWIMMING POOLS.
   (1)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      SWIMMING POOL. Any private recreational pool, pond, lake, or open tank
not located within a completely enclosed building and capable of containing
water to a depth at any point greater than 24 inches.
      SWIMMING POOL, ABOVE-GROUND. Any private recreational pool, pond, lake,
or open tank not located within a completely enclosed building and capable of
containing water to a depth at any point greater than 24 inches.
      SWIMMING POOL, IN-GROUND. A swimming pool whose sides are not exposed and
are below the natural ground located adjacent to said swimming pool.
      SWIMMING POOL, SURFACE. A swimming pool whose exposed sides have a height
of more than 24 inches, but less than four feet above the natural ground
located adjacent to said swimming pool.
   (2)   No swimming pool shall be allowed in any residential district unless
it complies with the following requirements.
      (A)   The pool is not operated as a business or private club.
      (B)   A pool shall not be located in any required front or required side
yard and shall not be closer than ten feet to any property line of the property
on which it is located. Pump and filter installations for pools shall not be
closer than 20 feet to any property line.
      (C)   For an in-ground or surface swimming pool, the pool or the property
upon which said pool is located shall be enclosed by a fence of a type which
effectively controls the entrance by children to the pool area. The fence must
be at least five feet in height. Wooden fences with boards placed vertically
shall not have any opening wider than four inches per opening and wooden fences
with boards placed horizontally shall not have any opening wider than one inch
per opening.
      (D)   Gates installed for access to the property or pool area shall be
equipped with a closing and latching device to protect against uncontrolled
access to the property.
      (E)   For an above-ground swimming pool, the pool shall be equipped with
an automatically retractable type ladder, a retractable ladder, a removable
ladder, or shall be fenced in. Said ladders must be removed when pool is not in
use.
      (F)   If access to the pool is via a deck or porch, then no access from
the ground is permitted to the deck area unless the property or the ground
access to the deck is fenced or gated.
      (G)   It shall be the responsibility of the property owner where said
pool is located to maintain all pool covers, fences, gates, and closure devices
in good operating condition.
      (H)   Hot tubs and spas will be excluded, providing a secured hard cover
when not in use.
   (3)   Swimming pools or ponds shall be approved by the Zoning Officer.
Above-ground pools constructed of light materials shall not require a
construction permit. All in-ground pools shall require a construction permit.
(Ord. 467, passed 9-14-2017) Penalty, see Sec.
153.999
Sec. 153.999 PENALTY.
   (1)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to Sec.
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (2)   Any person, firm, or corporation who violates, disobeys, omits,
neglects, or refuses to comply with any of the provisions of this chapter
shall, upon conviction, be fined not less than $10 nor more than $500 for each
offense. Each day that a violation is allowed to exist shall constitute a
separate offense.
(Prior Code, Sec. 21.26)
   (3)   Fines for violations of Sec.
153.129 shall be regulated by the Board of Trustees. Fines shall be not less
than $50 and not more than $500 per occurrence.
(Ord. 467, passed 9-14-2017)
